<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:intro-patterson}
		Let $\Gamma<\mathsf{G}$ be a Zariski-dense Borel--Anosov subgroup, and fix
		$(\varphi,\chi)\in\mathcal L^{>0}\times\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$. Let $\Gamma_0\lhd\Gamma$ be a non-elementary normal subgroup and let
		$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma_0,\mathbb{R})$ be invariant under conjugation by $\Gamma$.
		If $\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma_0)<\infty$, then
	there exists $\mu\in
		\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}
		\bigl(\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma_0),\Gamma_0\bigr)$
		such that
		$\operatorname{supp}\mu_x=\Lambda_\Gamma$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$.
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:A}
		Let $\Gamma<\mathsf{G}$ be a Zariski-dense Borel--Anosov subgroup, and fix
		$(\varphi,\chi)\in\mathcal L^{>0}\times\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$.  Then
		$0<\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)<\infty$,
		the triple $(\Gamma,\varphi,\chi)$ is of divergence
		type, and
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item There exists
			$\mu^{\varphi,\chi}\in
			\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}
			\bigl(\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma),\Gamma\bigr)$ with 
			$\operatorname{supp}\mu_x^{\varphi,\chi}=\Lambda_\Gamma$ for $x\in\mathbb X.$

			\item If
			$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\sigma,\Gamma)$ for some
			$\sigma>0$, then
			$\sigma=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$ and
			$\nu=c\,\mu^{\varphi,\chi}$ for some $c>0$.
			
			\item For every $x\in\mathbb X$,
			$\mu_x^{\varphi,\chi}$ is atomless and the
			$\Gamma$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_x^{\varphi,\chi})$
			is ergodic. Moreover,  
			$\Lambda_\Gamma^{\mathrm{con}}=\Lambda_\Gamma,$ and
			$\mu_x^{\varphi,\chi}
			(\mathcal F\smallsetminus\Lambda_\Gamma^{\mathrm{con}})=0.$
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:B}
		Let $\Gamma<\mathsf{G}$ be a Zariski-dense Borel--Anosov subgroup, and fix
		$(\varphi,\chi)\in\mathcal L^{>0}\times\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$.  Let $\Gamma_0\lhd\Gamma$ be a normal
		subgroup with $\Gamma_0\neq\{e\}$ and $\Gamma/\Gamma_0$ nilpotent. Then for every
		$\sigma>0$, the set of $\varphi$-conformal densities of $\Gamma_0$ of dimension $\sigma$
		which are ergodic with respect to $\Gamma_0$ equals the set of twisted
		$(\varphi,\chi)$-conformal densities of $\Gamma$ of dimension $\sigma$ for the
		characters $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ vanishing on $\Gamma_0$. Moreover the
		assignment $\mu\longmapsto\chi$ is a bijection between the set of ergodic
		$\varphi$-conformal densities of $\Gamma_0$ up to scalar multiples and the set of
		characters
		\[
		\big\{\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R}):\ \chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0\big\}\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma/\Gamma_0,\mathbb{R}).
		\]
		In particular, the set of dimensions of ergodic $\varphi$-conformal densities of
		$\Gamma_0$ is exactly $\{\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma):\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0\}$.
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:D}
		Let  $\Gamma<\mathsf{G}$ is a
		Zariski-dense Borel--Anosov subgroup. 
		Then $	\Theta_\Gamma\in\mathcal L_\Gamma^{>0}, \Theta_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}=\Theta_\Gamma,$ and $\delta_{\Theta_\Gamma}(\Gamma)=1.$
		Let $\{e\}\neq\Gamma_0\lhd\Gamma$ with
		$\Gamma/\Gamma_0$ nilpotent. Then:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item There is a unique, up to a positive scalar,
			$\Theta_\Gamma$-conformal density $\mu^\Theta$
			of dimension $1$ for $\Gamma$. Its measures are
			atomless, the $\Gamma$-action is ergodic and
			conservative, and they give full measure to
			$\Lambda^{\mathrm{con}}_{\Gamma}$. 
			
			\item The ergodic $\Theta_\Gamma$-conformal
			densities of $\Gamma_0$ of dimension $1$ are
			precisely the twisted
			$(\Theta_\Gamma,\chi)$-conformal densities of
			$\Gamma$ for characters
			$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma/\Gamma_0,\mathbb{R})$ satisfying
			$\delta_{\Theta_\Gamma,\chi}(\Gamma)=1$, and every
			occurring character satisfies
			\[
			|\chi(\gamma)|
			\leq\Theta_\Gamma(\lambda(\gamma))
			\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma).
			\]
			
			\item  $\mu^\Theta$
			is, up to a positive scalar, the unique
			ergodic $\Theta_\Gamma$-conformal density of
			$\Gamma_0$ of dimension $1$. In particular,
			the $\Gamma_0$-action on
			$(\mathcal F,\mu^\Theta_o)$ is ergodic.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:sv}
		Let $g,h\in \mathsf{G}$ and $1\leq i\leq d-1$. Then the following hold:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item  $\omega_i(\kappa(gh))\leq\omega_i(\kappa(g))+\omega_i(\kappa(h))$.
			\item 
			$\lVert\kappa(gh)-\kappa(h)\rVert\leq\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert$ and
			$\lVert\kappa(gh)-\kappa(g)\rVert\leq\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(h)\rVert$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
By \eqref{eq:wedge-norm} and the submultiplicativity of the
		operator norm,
		\[
		e^{\omega_i(\kappa(gh))}
		= \lVert \Lambda^i(gh)\rVert_{\operatorname{\mathrm{op}}}
		\leq \lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\operatorname{\mathrm{op}}}
		\lVert\Lambda^i h\rVert_{\operatorname{\mathrm{op}}}
		= e^{\omega_i(\kappa(g))+\omega_i(\kappa(h))},
		\]
		which proves~(1).
		
	For~(2), by \cite[Lemma~31.1]{CanaryAnosovNotes}, we have
	\(\sigma_j(g)\sigma_d(h)\leq\sigma_j(gh)
	\leq\sigma_j(g)\sigma_1(h)\) for every \(1\leq j\leq d\).
		Since \(\prod_{r=1}^d\sigma_r(h)=1\), we have
		\(\sigma_1(h)\geq1\geq\sigma_d(h)\), and hence
		\[
		\bigl|\log\sigma_j(gh)-\log\sigma_j(g)\bigr|
		\leq
		\max\{\log\sigma_1(h),-\log\sigma_d(h)\}
		\leq\lVert\kappa(h)\rVert .
		\]
	Summing the squares over
	\(j=1,\ldots,d\) yields
		\[
		\lVert\kappa(gh)-\kappa(g)\rVert
		\leq\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(h)\rVert .
		\]
			Finally, using \(\kappa(g^{-1})=\mathrm{i}\kappa(g)\) and the fact that
		\(\mathrm{i}\) is an isometry, we obtain
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\lVert\kappa(gh)-\kappa(h)\rVert
			&=\lVert\kappa(h^{-1}g^{-1})-\kappa(h^{-1})\rVert\\
			&\leq \sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g^{-1})\rVert
			=\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:product}
	Let $g,h\in\mathsf{G}$, let $1\leq i\leq d-1$, and suppose that
	$\alpha_i(\kappa(h))>0$. Choose the signs of
	$\mathbb V_{S_i(h)}$ and $\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}$ compatibly so that
	$\Lambda^i h\,\mathbb V_{S_i(h)}
	=
	e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}$.
	Then as operators on $\Lambda^i\mathbb{R}^d$, we have
	$\Lambda^i h
	=
	e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
	\big\langle\mathbb V_{S_i(h)},\cdot\big\rangle
	\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}
	+
	E_h$ with 
	$\lVert E_h\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
	\leq
	e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}$.
	Consequently, we have the following product estimate.
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:prod-two-sided}
	\begin{aligned}
		e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
		\left(
		\bigl\lVert\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}\bigr\rVert
		-e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
		\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\right)
		&\leq\lVert\Lambda^i(gh)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\\
		&\leq
		e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
		\left(
		\bigl\lVert\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}\bigr\rVert
		+e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
		\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\right).
	\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Set $N:=\binom{d}{i}$ and $T:=\Lambda^i h$, and write
		$\sigma_1(T)\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_N(T)$ for the singular values of $T$.
		By the singular-value formulas for exterior powers
		\cite[Appendix~A.5, (A.19)--(A.22)]{BPS19},
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\sigma_1(T)
			=\sigma_1(h)\cdots\sigma_i(h)
			=e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))},\quad
			\sigma_2(T)
			=\sigma_1(h)\cdots\sigma_{i-1}(h)\sigma_{i+1}(h)
			=e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Since $\alpha_i(\kappa(h))
		>0$ and 
		$\sigma_1(T)>\sigma_2(T)$, 
		the compatible choice of signs gives
		\[
	T\mathbb V_{S_i(h)}
	=
	\sigma_1(h)\cdots\sigma_i(h)\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}
	=
	\sigma_1(T)\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}.
	\]
		By the singular-value decomposition
		\cite[Corollary~2.6.7, p.~154]{HJ13}, this pair extends to
		orthonormal singular bases $\{v_r\}_{r=1}^N$ and
		$\{u_r\}_{r=1}^N$ satisfying $v_1=\mathbb V_{S_i(h)}$,
		$u_1=\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}$, and $Tv_r=\sigma_r(T)u_r$.
		Consequently,
		\[
			T
		=
		\sum_{r=1}^N
		\sigma_r(T)\langle v_r,\cdot\rangle u_r
		=
		e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
		\big\langle\mathbb V_{S_i(h)},\cdot\big\rangle
		\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}
		+E_h,\qquad 	E_h:=\sum_{r=2}^N\sigma_r(T)\langle v_r,\cdot\rangle u_r
		\]
		where $	\lVert E_h\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\leq\sigma_2(T)
		=e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}$.
		Applying $\Lambda^i g$ yields
		\[
		\Lambda^i(gh)
		=e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
		\big\langle\mathbb V_{S_i(h)},\cdot\big\rangle
		\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}
		+\Lambda^i gE_h.
		\]
	Moreover,
	\[
	\begin{split}
		\lVert\Lambda^i gE_h\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\leq
		\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\lVert E_h\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
		\leq
		e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
		\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.
	\end{split}
	\]
		The triangle and reverse triangle inequalities therefore give
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
			\left(
			\bigl\lVert\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}\bigr\rVert
			-e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
			\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
			\right)
			&\leq\lVert\Lambda^i(gh)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\\
			&\leq
			e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}
			\left(
			\bigl\lVert\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}\bigr\rVert
			+e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
			\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
			\right).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- dfn-1: body_md -->
\label{def:iwasawa}
	For $g\in \mathsf{G}$ and $\eta\in\mathcal F$, define 
	\[
	\sigma:\mathsf{G} \times\mathcal F\longrightarrow\mathesstixfrak a,
	\qquad
	\omega_i\bigl(\sigma(g,\eta)\bigr)
	:=
	\log\left\lVert\Lambda^i g\,\mathbb V_{\eta_i}\right\rVert,
	\qquad (1\leq i\leq d-1).
	\]
<!-- end dfn-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:sigma-props}
	For all $g,h\in\mathsf{G}$ and $\eta\in\mathcal F$, the following properties hold.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\sigma(gh,\eta)=\sigma(g,h\eta)+\sigma(h,\eta)$. Moreover,
		$\sigma(k,\eta)=0$ for every $k\in\mathsf{K}$.
		
		\item The map $\sigma$ is continuous.
		
		\item $\lVert\sigma(g,\eta)\rVert\leq
		\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert$.
		
		\item If all gaps of $\kappa(h)$ are positive, then, for every $i$,
		\[
		\Bigl\lvert
		\omega_i\bigl(\kappa(gh)\bigr)
		-\omega_i\bigl(\kappa(h)\bigr)
		-\omega_i\bigl(\sigma(g,U(h))\bigr)
		\Bigr\rvert
		\leq
		2e^{\,2\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
		\]
		provided
		$e^{\,2\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
		\leq\tfrac12$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Assertions~(1) and~(2) follow from the preceding discussion.
	For $k\in\mathsf{K}$, the $\mathsf{K}$-invariance gives
	\[
	\omega_i(\sigma(k,\eta))
	=\log\lVert\Lambda^i k\mathbb V_{\eta_i}\rVert=0
	\]
	for every $1\le i\le d-1$, and hence $\sigma(k,\eta)=0$. \textup{(3)} follows from
	\cite[Cor.~8.20(a)]{BQbook}. 
	For~(4), fix $i$ and set
	\[
	A_i:=\bigl\lVert\Lambda^i g\mathbb V_{U_i(h)}\bigr\rVert,
	\qquad
	t_i:=e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
	\frac{\lVert\Lambda^i g\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}}{A_i}.
	\]
	Dividing~\eqref{eq:prod-two-sided} by
	$e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}A_i$ gives
	\[
	1-t_i\leq
	\frac{\lVert\Lambda^i(gh)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}}
	{e^{\omega_i(\kappa(h))}A_i}
	\leq1+t_i.
	\]
	By Cauchy--Schwarz inequality,
\[
 \omega_i(\kappa(g)) =\Bigl|\sum_{j=1}^i \log\sigma_j(g)\Bigr| \leq\sqrt{i}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^i (\log\sigma_j(g))^2\Bigr)^{1/2} \leq\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert. 
 \]
	Hence, by~\eqref{eq:wedge-norm},
	\[
	t_i
	\leq
	e^{-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))
		+\omega_i(\kappa(g))
		+\omega_i(\mathrm{i}\kappa(g))}
	\leq
	e^{2\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))}
	=:\varepsilon_i.
	\]
	Under the stated assumption, $t_i\leq\varepsilon_i\leq\frac12$.
	Since $|\log(1\pm t)|\leq2t$ for $0\leq t\leq\frac12$, taking
	logarithms and using~\eqref{eq:wedge-norm} and Definition~\ref{def:iwasawa} yields
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\bigl|\omega_i(\kappa(gh))-\omega_i(\kappa(h))
		-\omega_i(\sigma(g,U(h)))\bigr|
		&\leq 2t_i\\
		&\leq
		2e^{2\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(g)\rVert-\alpha_i(\kappa(h))},
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	as required.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- dfn-2: body_md -->
\label{def:busemann}
	For $\eta\in\mathcal F$, define
	\[
	\beta_\eta\colon \mathbb X\times \mathbb X\longrightarrow\mathesstixfrak a,
	\qquad
	\beta_\eta(g\mathsf{K},h\mathsf{K})
	:=\sigma(g^{-1},\eta)-\sigma(h^{-1},\eta).
	\]
<!-- end dfn-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:beta-props}
	For all $g,g_1,g_2,g_3\in\mathsf{G}$ and $\eta\in\mathcal F$, the following hold.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item  $\beta_\eta(g_1\mathsf{K},g_3\mathsf{K})
		=\beta_\eta(g_1\mathsf{K},g_2\mathsf{K})+\beta_\eta(g_2\mathsf{K},g_3\mathsf{K}),$ and  $	\beta_{g\eta}(gg_1\mathsf{K},gg_2\mathsf{K})=\beta_\eta(g_1\mathsf{K},g_2\mathsf{K}).$
		\item  $\beta_\eta(g\mathsf{K},h\mathsf{K})
		=\sigma(g^{-1}h,h^{-1}\eta)$
		\item $\lVert\beta_\eta(g\mathsf{K},h\mathsf{K})\rVert
		\leq\lVert \kappa(g\mathsf{K},h\mathsf{K})\rVert$.
		\item  The map $\eta\longmapsto \beta_\eta(g\mathsf{K}, h\mathsf{K})$ is continuous on $\mathcal F$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- dfn-3: body_md -->
\label{def:gromov}
	Define
	\[
	\mathcal G:\mathcal F^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathesstixfrak a,\qquad
	\omega_i\bigl(\mathcal G(\xi,\eta)\bigr)
	:=-\log\bigl\lVert
	\mathbb V_{\xi_i}\wedge\mathbb V_{\eta_{d-i}}
	\bigr\rVert,
	\quad (1\leq i\leq d-1).
	\]
<!-- end dfn-3 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:gromov-equiv}
	For every $g\in\mathsf{G}$ and $(\xi,\eta)\in\mathcal F^{(2)}$,
	\[
	\mathcal G(g\xi,g\eta)
	=
	\mathcal G(\xi,\eta)+\sigma(g,\xi)+\mathrm{i}\sigma(g,\eta).
	\]
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
This is \cite[Lem.~4.12]{Sam}, in the above conventions.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:dphi-qi}
	The following assertions hold.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item There exist $A\geq1$ and $B\geq0$, depending only on
		$(\Gamma,S,\varphi)$, such that, for all $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
		\[
		A^{-1}d_w(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)-B
		\leq d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
		\leq A\,d_w(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)+B.
		\]
		
		\item For all $\gamma_1,\gamma_2,\gamma_3\in\Gamma$,
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\bigl|d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_3)-d_\varphi(\gamma_2,\gamma_3)\bigr|
			&\leq
			\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert
			\lVert\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2)\rVert,\\
			\bigl|d_\varphi(\gamma_3,\gamma_1)-d_\varphi(\gamma_3,\gamma_2)\bigr|
			&\leq
			\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert
			\lVert\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2)\rVert.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		In particular, both left-hand sides are at most
		$\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert
		L(d_w(\gamma_1,\gamma_2))$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Assertion \textup{(1)} is precisely \cite[Lem.~4.5]{DOT}.
	For \textup{(2)}, left invariance reduces to the case $\gamma_3=e$.
	Since
	$\gamma_1^{-1}=(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2)\gamma_2^{-1}$,
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sv}\textup{(2)} gives
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\bigl|d_\varphi(\gamma_1,e)-d_\varphi(\gamma_2,e)\bigr|
		&=
		\bigl|\varphi\bigl(
		\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1})-\kappa(\gamma_2^{-1})
		\bigr)\bigr|\\
		&\leq
		\lVert\varphi\rVert
		\bigl\lVert
		\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1})-\kappa(\gamma_2^{-1})
		\bigr\rVert\\
		&\leq
		\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert
		\bigl\lVert\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2)\bigr\rVert.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
The second inequality follows similarly by applying
Lemma~\ref{lem:sv}\textup{(2)} to
\(\gamma_1=\gamma_2(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_1)\), and using
\eqref{eq:kappa-inverse} together with the \(\iota\)-invariance of the norm.
The final bound in both cases follows from the definition of \(L\) in
\eqref{eq:L}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:coarse-add}
		There exists $D_0=D_0(\Gamma,S)\geq0$ such that, whenever
		$\gamma_2$ lies on a word geodesic from $\gamma_1$ to $\gamma_3$,
		\[
		\bigl\lVert
		\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_3)
		-\kappa(\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2)
		-\kappa(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_3)
		\bigr\rVert
		\leq D_0.
		\]
		In particular, with $D_\varphi:=\lVert\varphi\rVert D_0$,
		\[
		\bigl|
		d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_3)
		-d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
		-d_\varphi(\gamma_2,\gamma_3)
		\bigr|
		\leq D_\varphi.
		\]
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
The geodesic assumption gives
		$|\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_3|
		=|\gamma_1^{-1}\gamma_2|+|\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_3|$; hence the first
		assertion follows from \cite[Cor.~5.15]{LO}, and the second follows
		by applying $\varphi$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:buslimit}
	Let $x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$ and let $(\gamma_n)$ be a sequence in $\Gamma$ converging
	to $x$ in $\overline{\Gamma}$. Then, for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$,
	\[
	\kappa(\gamma^{-1}\gamma_n)-\kappa(\gamma_n)
	\longrightarrow
	\sigma\bigl(\gamma^{-1},\theta(x)\bigr)
	\qquad(n\to\infty).
	\]
	Consequently, for every $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
	\[
	\kappa(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_n\mathsf{K})
	-\kappa(\gamma_2\mathsf{K},\gamma_n\mathsf{K})
	\longrightarrow
	\beta_{\theta(x)}(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_2\mathsf{K})
	\qquad(n\to\infty).
	\]
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Since $\gamma_n\to x$ in $\overline{\Gamma}$, we have
	$|\gamma_n|\to\infty$; hence \eqref{eq:anosovgap} gives
	\[
	\min_{1\leq i\leq d-1}
	\alpha_i\bigl(\kappa(\gamma_n)\bigr)
	\longrightarrow\infty.
	\]
	Fix $\gamma\in\Gamma$. By
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sigma-props}\textup{(4)}, for every $i$ and all
	sufficiently large $n$,
	\[
	\left|
	\omega_i\!\left(
	\kappa(\gamma^{-1}\gamma_n)-\kappa(\gamma_n)
	-\sigma(\gamma^{-1},U(\gamma_n))
	\right)
	\right|
	\leq
	2e^{\,2\sqrt d\,\lVert\kappa(\gamma^{-1})\rVert
		-\alpha_i(\kappa(\gamma_n))}
	\longrightarrow0.
	\]
	By \eqref{eq:cartan-property} and
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sigma-props}\textup{(2)}, we obtain 
	\[
	\sigma(\gamma^{-1},U(\gamma_n))
	\longrightarrow
	\sigma(\gamma^{-1},\theta(x))
	\qquad(n\to\infty).
	\]
	Together with the preceding estimates, this proves the first assertion.
	              
Consequently, applying the first assertion with $\gamma=\gamma_j$, $j=1,2$, we obtain, by
Definition~\ref{def:busemann},
\[
\begin{aligned}
	\kappa(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_n\mathsf{K})-\kappa(\gamma_2\mathsf{K},\gamma_n\mathsf{K})
	&\longrightarrow
	\sigma(\gamma_1^{-1},\theta(x))
	-\sigma(\gamma_2^{-1},\theta(x)) \qquad(n\to\infty)\\
	&=\beta_{\theta(x)}(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_2\mathsf{K}).
\end{aligned}
\]
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{Prop:busctrl}
	For every $R\geq 0$, set
	\[
	A_R:=D_\varphi+2\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,L(R).
	\]
	Then, for all $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$ and every
	$x\in\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$,
	\[
	\left|
	\varphi\bigl(\beta_{\theta(x)}
	(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_2\mathsf{K})\bigr)
	-d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
	\right|
	\leq A_R.
	\]
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Choose a geodesic ray $c$ from $\gamma_1$ to $p$ meeting
	$B_w(\gamma_2,R)$, and let $\gamma:=c(t_0)$ be such that
	$d_w(u,\gamma_2)\leq R$. Set $\gamma_n:=c(n)$ for $n\geq t_0$, so
	that $\gamma_n\to x$. 
	Applying $\varphi$ to the second assertion of Proposition~\ref{prop:buslimit} and using \eqref{Eq:d}, we obtain
	\[
	d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_n)-d_\varphi(\gamma_2,\gamma_n)
	\longrightarrow
	\varphi\bigl(\beta_{\theta(x)}
	(\gamma_1\mathsf{K},\gamma_2\mathsf{K})\bigr)
	\qquad(n\to\infty).
	\]
	Fix $n\geq t_0$. Since $\gamma$ lies on the geodesic segment from
	$\gamma_1$ to $\gamma_n$, Proposition~\ref{prop:coarse-add} gives
	\[
	\left|
	d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_n)-d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma)-d_\varphi(\gamma,\gamma_n)
	\right|
	\leq D_\varphi.
	\]
	Moreover, Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}\textup{(2)} and
	$d_w(\gamma,\gamma_2)\leq R$ yield
	\[
	\left|
	d_\varphi(\gamma_2,\gamma_n)-d_\varphi(\gamma,\gamma_n)
	\right|
	\leq \sqrt{d}\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,L(R)
	\]
	and
	\[
	\left|
	d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma)-d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
	\right|
	\leq \sqrt{d}\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,L(R).
	\]
	Combining these estimates, we obtain
	\[
	\left|
	d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_n)-d_\varphi(\gamma_2,\gamma_n)
	-d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
	\right|
	\leq A_R.
	\]
	By Proposition~\ref{prop:buslimit}, letting $n\to\infty$ in the preceding estimate yields the assertion.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- dfn-4: body_md -->
\label{def:word-shadows}
	For $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$ and $R\geq0$, define the shadow
	\[
	\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
	:=
	\left\{
	\xi\in\partial_\infty\Gamma:
	\text{some geodesic ray $[\gamma_1,\xi)$ meets
		$B_w(\gamma_2,R)$}
	\right\}.
	\]
<!-- end dfn-4 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:shadow-basic}
	Let $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$, $R\geq0$.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$ is a closed subset of $\partial_\infty\Gamma$, non-decreasing in $R$, and
		$\gamma\,\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)=\mathcal O_R(\gamma\gamma_1,\gamma\gamma_2)$ for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$.
		\item  If $c$ is a geodesic ray from $\gamma_1$ to $x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$,
		then $x\in\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,c(t))$ for all $t\geq0$, $R\geq0$.
		\item If $d_w(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\leq R$ then $\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)=\partial_\infty\Gamma$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:multiplicity}
		For every $R\geq 0$ there exists $M_R\geq 1$ such that, for all
		$m\in\mathbb{R}$ and $x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$,
		\[
		\left|\left\{\gamma\in\Gamma:
		x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma),\
		m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1
		\right\}\right|
		\leq M_R.
		\]
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Suppose $x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\cap\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma')$ with
		$d_\varphi(e,\gamma),d_\varphi(e,\gamma')\in[m,m+1)$. Choose geodesic rays
		$c,c'$ from $e$ to $x$ such that $c$ meets $B_w(\gamma,R)$ at
		$\gamma_1=c(t_1)$ and $c'$ meets $B_w(\gamma',R)$ at
		$\widetilde{\gamma}_2=c'(t_2)$. Since $c$ and $c'$ have the same initial point and the same endpoint at infinity, by \cite[Lem.~III.H.3.3(1)]{BH}, we have
		$d_w\bigl(c(t_2),c'(t_2)\bigr)\leq 2\delta_0$.
		Setting $\gamma_2:=c(t_2)$, we obtain
		\[
		d_w(\gamma_2,\gamma')
		\leq d_w(\gamma_2,\widetilde{\gamma}_2)
		+d_w(\widetilde{\gamma}_2,\gamma')
		\leq 2\delta_0+R.
		\]
		Thus, $c$ meets $B_w(\gamma',2\delta_0+R)$ at $\gamma_2$. By
		Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}\textup{(2)}, with
		$q(R):=\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,L(2\delta_0+R)$,
		we have
		\[
		d_\varphi(e,\gamma_1), d_\varphi(e, \gamma_2)\in
		\bigl[m-q(R),m+1+q(R)\bigr].
		\]
		Assume $t_1\leq t_2$, the other case being identical. Then $\gamma_1$
		lies on the geodesic $c([0,t_2])$ from $e$ to $\gamma_2$, so
		Proposition~\ref{prop:coarse-add} gives
		\[
		d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
		\leq d_\varphi(e,\gamma_2)-d_\varphi(e,\gamma_1)+D_\varphi
		\leq 1+2q(R)+D_\varphi.
		\]
		Hence, by Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}\textup{(1)},
		\[
		d_w(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)
		\leq A\bigl(1+2q(R)+D_\varphi+B\bigr)
		=:C(R).
		\]
		Consequently, we obtain
		\[
		d_w(\gamma,\gamma')
		\leq R+C(R)+R+2\delta_0.
		\]
		Thus, all such $\gamma'$ lie in the ball of radius
		$2R+2\delta_0+C(R)$ about $\gamma$, and we may take
		\[
		M_R:=\left|B_w\bigl(e,2R+2\delta_0+C(R)\bigr)\right|.
		\]
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:concentration}
	There exists $c_2=c_2(\delta_0)\geq0$ such that, for all
	$\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$, $R\geq0$, and
	$x,y\in\partial_\infty\Gamma\smallsetminus\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$, one has
	$(x\mid y)_{\gamma_2}\geq R-c_2$. Consequently,
	\[
	\sup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
	\operatorname{diam}_\varrho\bigl(\partial_\infty\Gamma\smallsetminus\mathcal O_R(\gamma,e)\bigr)
	\leq
	C_0e^{-\varepsilon_0(R-c_2)}
	\longrightarrow 0
	\qquad (R\to\infty),
	\]
	with the convention $\operatorname{diam}_\varrho\emptyset=0$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
If $x,y\notin\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$, choose
$\gamma_n\in[\gamma_1,x)$ such that $\gamma_n\to x$. Then
$d_w(\gamma_2,[\gamma_1,\gamma_n])>R$. By the proof that \textup{(1)} implies \textup{(3)} in
\cite[Prop.~III.H.1.17]{BH} and the observation following
\cite[Def.~III.H.1.19]{BH}, one has
 $(\gamma_n\mid\gamma_1)_{\gamma_2}\geq R-4\delta_0$. Hence, 
 $(x\mid\gamma_1)_{\gamma_2}\geq R-4\delta_0$ by \eqref{Eq:Ext}. The same argument
 with $y$ in place of $x$, we obtain
 $(y\mid\gamma_1)_{\gamma_2}
 \geq R-4\delta_0$. Applying \cite[Rem.~III.H.3.17(4)]{BH}, we obtain the first assertion. The second assertion
 follows from \eqref{eq:visual} by taking $\gamma_1=\gamma$ and
 $\gamma_2=e$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:shrinking}
		For every $\gamma\in\Gamma$ and $R\geq 0$, if
		$x,y\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)$, then
		$(x\mid y)_e\geq\lvert\gamma\rvert-R-3\delta_0$; consequently,
		$\operatorname{diam}_\varrho\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\leq
		C_0e^{-\varepsilon_0(\lvert\gamma\rvert-R-3\delta_0)}$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Choose a geodesic ray $[e,x)$ meeting $B_w(\gamma,R)$ at $u$ and a sequence $\gamma_n\in[e,x)$ converging to $x$ such that $u\in[e,\gamma_n]$ for every $n$. Since $d_w(u,\gamma)\leq R$, we have $|u|\geq|\gamma|-R$, while $d_w(\gamma_n,\gamma)\leq d_w(\gamma_n,u)+R=|\gamma_n|-|u|+R$. Consequently, $(\gamma_n\mid\gamma)_e\geq\frac12(|u|+|\gamma|-R)\geq|\gamma|-R$, and hence $(x\mid\gamma)_e\geq|\gamma|-R$ by \eqref{Eq:Ext}. Similarly, $(y\mid\gamma)_e\geq|\gamma|-R$. Thus, \cite[Rem.~III.H.3.17(4)]{BH} yields $(x\mid y)_e\geq\min\{(x\mid\gamma)_e,(\gamma\mid y)_e\}-2\delta_0\geq|\gamma|-R-2\delta_0\geq|\gamma|-R-3\delta_0$. The diameter estimate follows from \eqref{eq:visual}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:engulfing}
		There exists $R_1=R_1(\delta_0)\geq1$ such that, for all $R\geq R_1$ and $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$, if $\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_1)\cap\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_2)\neq\emptyset$ and $\lvert\gamma_2\rvert\geq\lvert\gamma_1\rvert$, then $\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_2)\subseteq\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma_1)$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Fix $x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_1)\cap\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_2)$ and
	$y\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_2)$. By the proof of
	Lemma~\ref{lem:shrinking}, we obtain
	$(x\mid\gamma_1)_e\geq\lvert\gamma_1\rvert-R$ and
	$(x\mid\gamma_2)_e,(y\mid\gamma_2)_e\geq
	\lvert\gamma_2\rvert-R$. Applying
	\cite[Rem.~III.H.3.17(4)]{BH} twice and using
	$\lvert\gamma_2\rvert\geq\lvert\gamma_1\rvert$, we obtain
	$(\gamma_1\mid\gamma_2)_e\geq
	\lvert\gamma_1\rvert-R-2\delta_0$ and hence
	$(y\mid\gamma_1)_e\geq
	\lvert\gamma_1\rvert-R-4\delta_0$. Let $[\smash{e,y})$ be a geodesic ray and choose
	$\gamma_n\in[e,y)$ with $\gamma_n$ converging to $y$. By
	\cite[Rem.~III.H.3.17(5)]{BH},
	$\liminf_{n\to\infty}(\gamma_n\mid\gamma_1)_e\geq
	\lvert\gamma_1\rvert-R-6\delta_0$. Moreover, the proof of
	\cite[Prop.~III.H.1.17, (1)$\Rightarrow$(3)]{BH}, together
	with the observation following
	\cite[Def.~III.H.1.19]{BH}, gives
	$d_w(\gamma_1,[e,\gamma_n])\leq
	(e\mid\gamma_n)_{\gamma_1}+4\delta_0
	=\lvert\gamma_1\rvert-(\gamma_n\mid\gamma_1)_e+4\delta_0$.
	Letting $n\to\infty$, we obtain 
	 $d_w(\gamma_1,[e,y))\leq R+10\delta_0$. Therefore, for
	$R\geq R_1:=2\delta_0$, one has
	$d_w(\gamma_1,[e,y))\leq6R$, and hence
	$y\in\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma_1)$. Since $y$ was arbitrary, the
	assertion follows.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:vitali}
		Let $R\geq R_1$ and $I\subseteq\Gamma$. Then there exists
		$J\subseteq I$ such that the shadows
		$\{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\}_{\gamma\in J}$ are pairwise disjoint and
		\[
		\bigcup_{\gamma\in I}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)
		\subseteq
		\bigcup_{\gamma\in J}\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma).
		\]
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
If $I=\varnothing$, take $J=\varnothing$. Otherwise, enumerate
		$I=\{\gamma_1,\gamma_2,\ldots\}$ so that
		\[
		|\gamma_1|\leq|\gamma_2|\leq\cdots,
		\]
		using a finite enumeration when $I$ is finite. This is possible
		since word balls are finite. Construct $J$ inductively: include
		$\gamma_n$ if and only if $\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_n)$ is disjoint from
		$\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_m)$ for every $m<n$ with $\gamma_m\in J$.
		The selected shadows are pairwise disjoint by construction.
		  
		If $\gamma_n\notin J$, there exists $m<n$ with $\gamma_m\in J$
		such that $\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_n)\cap\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_m)\neq\varnothing.$
		Since $|\gamma_m|\leq|\gamma_n|$, Lemma~\ref{lem:engulfing} gives
		$\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_n)\subseteq\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma_m).$
		If $\gamma_n\in J$, then
		$\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_n)\subseteq\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma_n)$.
		Taking unions yields
		\[
		\bigcup_{\gamma\in I}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)
		\subseteq
		\bigcup_{\gamma\in J}\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma),
		\]
		as required.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- dfn-5: body_md -->
\label{def:twisted}
		Let $\delta>0$. A \emph{twisted $(\varphi,\chi)$-conformal density of dimension
			$\delta$ for $\mathsf{H}$} is a family $\nu=(\nu_x)_{x\in \mathbb X}$ of finite non-zero Borel measures
		on $\mathcal F$, all supported on $\Lambda_\Gamma$, such that for all $x,y\in \mathbb X$, $h\in \mathsf{H}$:
		\begin{align}
			\nu_x&=e^{-\delta\,\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}\,\nu_y
			&&\text{(conformality)},\label{eq:conf}\\
			h_\ast\nu_x&=e^{-\chi(h)}\,\nu_{hx}
			&&\text{(twisted equivariance)}.\label{eq:equiv}
		\end{align}
		We write $\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ for the set of such families. Denote 
		$\mathcal M_\varphi(\delta,\mathsf{H}):=\mathcal M_{\varphi,0}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ and call its elements \emph{$\varphi$-conformal densities of dimension
			$\delta$ for $\mathsf{H}$}.
<!-- end dfn-5 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:density-basic}
		Let $\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item $\lVert\nu_{hx}\rVert=e^{\chi(h)}\lVert\nu_x\rVert$ for $h\in\mathsf{H}$, $x\in\mathbb X$.
			\item For every Borel $A\subseteq\mathcal F$ and $x,y\in\mathbb X$,
			$\displaystyle \nu_x(A)=\int_A e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(x,y))}\,d\nu_y(\xi)$.
			\item For $h\in\mathsf{H}$ and $x\in\mathbb X$,
			$\displaystyle \frac{d(h_\ast\nu_x)}{d\nu_x}(\xi)
			= e^{-\chi(h)}\,e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(hx,\,x))}$, a positive continuous
			function of $\xi\in\mathcal F$.
			\item $\operatorname{supp}\nu_x=\Lambda_\Gamma$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$.
			\item $\lvert\log\lVert\nu_x\rVert-\log\lVert\nu_y\rVert\rvert
			\leq\delta\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,\lVert\kappa(x,y)\rVert$ for all $x,y\in\mathbb X$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
(1) By \eqref{eq:equiv}, we have $\lVert\nu_x\rVert=\lVert h_\ast\nu_x\rVert
=e^{-\chi(h)}\lVert\nu_{hx}\rVert$.
		
		(2) This is precisely the integral form of \eqref{eq:conf}.
		
	(3) Combining \eqref{eq:equiv} and \eqref{eq:conf}, we obtain
	$h_\ast\nu_x=e^{-\chi(h)}\nu_{hx}
	=e^{-\chi(h)}e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(hx,x))}\nu_x$,
	which immediately yields the asserted Radon--Nikodym derivative. 
	
(4) By \eqref{eq:conf}, $\operatorname{supp}\nu_x$ is independent of $x$. It is a
non-empty closed subset of $\Lambda_\Gamma$ and is $\mathsf{H}$-invariant by
\eqref{eq:equiv}. Since $\theta\colon\partial_\infty\Gamma\to\Lambda_\Gamma$ is an
$\mathsf{H}$-equivariant homeomorphism, $\theta^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}\nu_x)$ is a
non-empty closed $\mathsf{H}$-invariant subset of $\partial_\infty\Gamma$. By
\cite[Prop.~12.1(1) and Thm.~12.2(5)]{KB}, we obtain $\partial_\infty\Gamma\subseteq\theta^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}\nu_x)$.
Therefore, $\operatorname{supp}\nu_x=\Lambda_\Gamma$.
		    
		
	(5) By (2) and Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(3), we have
	\[
	e^{-\delta\lVert\varphi\rVert\lVert\kappa(x,y)\rVert}
	\leq
	\frac{\lVert\nu_x\rVert}{\lVert\nu_y\rVert}
	\leq
	e^{\delta\lVert\varphi\rVert\lVert\kappa(x,y)\rVert}.
	\]
	Taking logarithms proves (5).
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- dfn-6: body_md -->
For $\gamma\in\Gamma$ and $\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$, define
	\[
	\nu^{[\gamma]}_x
	:=\frac{1}{\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}\,(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma x}
	\qquad (x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
<!-- end dfn-6 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:translates}
	For $\gamma\in\Gamma$ and $\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$, $\nu^{[\gamma]}\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ and
	$\lVert\nu^{[\gamma]}_o\rVert=1$. Moreover, for every $R\geq0$ and
	$\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:fullness-translation}
		\frac{\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)}
		{\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert}
		=
		\nu^{[\gamma_2]}_o
		\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_1,e)\bigr).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By definition and the $\Gamma$-invariance of $\Lambda_\Gamma$, each
	$\nu^{[\gamma]}_x$ is a finite non-zero Borel measure supported on $\Lambda_\Gamma$. 
	To verify conformality, let $x,y\in\mathbb X$ and $\xi\in\mathcal F$. By the
	definition of $\nu^{[\gamma]}$, the conformality of $\nu$, we have
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\frac{d\nu^{[\gamma]}_x}{d\nu^{[\gamma]}_y}(\xi)
		=
		\frac{
			d\bigl(\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert^{-1}
			(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma x}\bigr)
		}{
			d\bigl(\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert^{-1}
			(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma y}\bigr)
		}(\xi)
		=
		\frac{d\bigl((\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma x}\bigr)}
		{d\bigl((\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma y}\bigr)}(\xi)
		=
		\frac{d\nu_{\gamma x}}{d\nu_{\gamma y}}(\gamma\xi)
		=
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_{\gamma\xi}(\gamma x,\gamma y))},
	\end{aligned}
	\]
which is equal to $e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(x,y))}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(1).
   
For $h\in\mathsf{H}$, normality of $\mathsf{H}$ and \eqref{eq:equiv} give
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		h_\ast\nu^{[g]}_x
		=
		\frac{1}{\lVert\nu_{go}\rVert}
		(g^{-1})_\ast(ghg^{-1})_\ast\nu_{gx}
		=
		\frac{e^{-\chi(ghg^{-1})}}{\lVert\nu_{go}\rVert}
		(g^{-1})_\ast\nu_{ghx}
		=
		e^{-\chi(h)}\nu^{[g]}_{hx},
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	where the last equality follows from \eqref{eq:conj-inv}. Hence
	$\nu^{[g]}\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$. Since push-forward
	preserves total mass,
	\[
	\lVert\nu^{[\gamma]}_o\rVert
	=
	\frac{\lVert(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}
	{\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}
	=1.
	\]
	Finally, by Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow-basic}(1),
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\nu^{[\gamma_2]}_o
		\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_1,e)\bigr)
		&=
		\frac{1}{\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert}
		\bigl((\gamma_2^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigr)
		\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_1,e)\bigr)\\
		&=
		\frac{\nu_{\gamma_2o}
			\bigl(\gamma_2\mathcal O_R(\gamma_2^{-1}\gamma_1,e)\bigr)}
		{\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert}\\
		&=
		\frac{\nu_{\gamma_2o}
			\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)}
		{\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	This proves \eqref{eq:fullness-translation}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- dfn-7: body_md -->
\label{def:normalised-class}
	We denote by $\mathcal D_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ the collection of
	normalised base-point measures arising from twisted
	$(\varphi,\chi)$-conformal densities:
	\[
	\mathcal D_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})
	:=
	\left\{
	\nu_o:\ 
	\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H}),\
	\lVert\nu_o\rVert=1
	\right\}.
	\]
	For simplicity, we write
	$\mathcal D:=\mathcal D_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
<!-- end dfn-7 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:compact-class}
	The set $\mathcal D$ is closed in $\mathcal P(\mathcal F)$, and hence compact.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let
		$\nu_n=(\nu_{n,x})_{x\in\mathbb X}
		\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$
		satisfy $\lVert\nu_{n,o}\rVert=1$, and suppose that
		$\nu_{n,o}\to\sigma$ weak-$\ast$ in $\mathcal P(\mathcal F)$.
		Hence $\sigma$ is a probability
		measure with $\operatorname{supp}\sigma\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$.
		              
	 For $x\in\mathbb X$, define $\sigma_x
		:=
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,o))}\sigma.$
		       
	It follows directly from Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(4) that
	each $\sigma_x$ is a finite non-zero Borel measure and that $\operatorname{supp}\sigma_x=\operatorname{supp}\sigma\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$.
		      
		By the cocycle property in Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(1), for all
		$x,y\in\mathbb X$,
		\[
		\sigma_x
		=
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(y,o))}\sigma
		=
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}\sigma_y.
		\]
	Thus $(\sigma_x)_{x\in\mathbb X}$
		satisfies \eqref{eq:conf}.
		 Since
		$\nu_n\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$, combining
		\eqref{eq:conf} and \eqref{eq:equiv}, gives, for every
		$h\in\mathsf{H}$ and $f\in \mathcal C(\mathcal F)$,
		\[
		\int_{\mathcal F}f(h\xi)\,d\nu_{n,o}(\xi)
		=
		\int_{\mathcal F}
		f(\xi)e^{-\chi(h)}
		e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(ho,o))}
		\,d\nu_{n,o}(\xi).
		\]
		Since both integrands are continuous,  passing to the weak-$\ast$
		limit, we obtain
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\int_{\mathcal F}f(\xi)\,d(h_\ast\sigma)(\xi)
			&=
			\int_{\mathcal F}f(h\xi)\,d\sigma(\xi)\\
			&=
			\int_{\mathcal F}
			f(\xi)e^{-\chi(h)}
			e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(ho,o))}
			\,d\sigma(\xi)\\
			&=
			e^{-\chi(h)}
			\int_{\mathcal F}f(\xi)\,d\sigma_{ho}(\xi).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Since this holds for every $f\in \mathcal C(\mathcal F)$, it implies
			\[
		h_\ast\sigma=e^{-\chi(h)}\sigma_{ho}
		\qquad (h\in\mathsf{H}).
		\]
		Using the equivariance of $\beta$ from
		Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(1) and the conformality established above,
		 we obtain
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			h_\ast\sigma_x
			&=
			e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(hx,ho))}\,h_\ast\sigma\\
			&=
			e^{-\chi(h)}
			e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(hx,ho))}\sigma_{ho}\\
			&=
			e^{-\chi(h)}\sigma_{hx},
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		for every $h\in\mathsf{H}$ and $x\in\mathbb X$.
		Hence $(\sigma_x)_{x\in\mathbb X}\in
		\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$. Since
		$\beta_\bullet(o,o)=0$, we have $\sigma_o=\sigma$, and hence
		$\lVert\sigma_o\rVert=1$. Therefore $\sigma\in\mathcal D$, proving that
		$\mathcal D$ is weak-$\ast$ closed in $\mathcal P(\mathcal F)$. Since $\mathcal F$ is
		compact, $\mathcal P(\mathcal F)$ is weak-$\ast$ compact, and consequently
		so is $\mathcal D$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:fullness}
		There exist
		$R_2=R_2(\varphi,\chi,\delta,\mathsf{H})\geq R_1$ and
		$m_0\in(0,1]$ such that, for every
		$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$, every $R\geq R_2$,
		and all $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
		\[
		\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
		\geq
		m_0\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert.
		\]
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By \eqref{eq:fullness-translation} and
		Lemma~\ref{lem:translates}, it is enough to find
		$R_2\geq R_1$ and $m_0>0$ such that $\sigma\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma,e)\bigr)\geq m_0$
		for every $\sigma\in\mathcal D$, $\gamma\in\Gamma$, and $R\geq R_2$.
		Suppose, to the contrary, that there exist sequences
		$\sigma_n\in\mathcal D$, $\gamma_n\in\Gamma$, and $R_n\to\infty$ such that $\sigma_n\bigl(\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)\bigr)\rightarrow0.$
	    
		If $\lvert\gamma_n\rvert\leq R_n$ for infinitely many $n$,
		then Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow-basic}(3) gives
		$\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)=\partial_\infty\Gamma$, contradicting
		$\sigma_n(\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e))\to0$. Therefore, $\lvert\gamma_n\rvert>R_n$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
		      
		Set $K_n:=\partial_\infty\Gamma\smallsetminus\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)$. 
		Since
		$\theta(K_n)=\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus
		\theta(\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e))$
		and $\sigma_n(\Lambda_\Gamma)=1$, we have
	\[
	\sigma_n\bigl(\theta(K_n)\bigr)
	=
1
	-\sigma_n\bigl(\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)\bigr)
	\longrightarrow 1
	\qquad (n\to\infty).
	\]
	Thus we have
		$\sigma_n(\theta(K_n))>\frac12$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
		If $K_n=\varnothing$, then $\theta(K_n)=\varnothing$, and hence
		$\sigma_n(\theta(K_n))=0$, a contradiction. Therefore,
		$K_n\neq\varnothing$ for all sufficiently large $n$, and we may
		choose $q_n\in K_n$.
		By Lemma~\ref{lem:concentration},
		\[
		K_n\subseteq\overline B_\rho(q_n,\varepsilon_n),
		\qquad
		\varepsilon_n
		:=
		C_0e^{-\varepsilon_0(R_n-c_2)}
		\longrightarrow0.
		\]
		After passing to a subsequence, compactness of $\partial_\infty\Gamma$ and
		Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-class} give $q_n\longrightarrow q\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$ and $\sigma_n\longrightarrow\sigma\in\mathcal D$  weak-$\ast$.
		Let $r>0$ and let $f\in \mathcal C(\mathcal F)$ satisfy $0\leq f\leq1$ and
		$f=0$ on $\theta(\overline B_\rho(q,r))$. For all sufficiently
		large $n$, we have 
		$K_n
		\subseteq
		\overline B_\rho(q_n,\varepsilon_n)
		\subseteq
		\overline B_\rho(q,r).$
		Hence $f$ vanishes on $\theta(K_n)$, and therefore
		\[
		0\leq
		\int_{\mathcal F} f\,d\sigma_n
		=
		\int_{\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)} f\,d\sigma_n
		\leq
		\sigma_n\bigl(\mathcal O_{R_n}(\gamma_n,e)\bigr)
		\longrightarrow 0.
		\]
		The weak-$\ast$ convergence gives
		$\int_{\mathcal F}f\,d\sigma=0$. Since $r$ and $f$ are arbitrary and
		$\operatorname{supp}\sigma\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$, it follows that
		\[
		\operatorname{supp}\sigma\subseteq
		\bigcap_{r>0}\theta\bigl(\overline B_\rho(q,r)\bigr)
		=
		\{\theta(q)\}.
		\]
		Thus $\sigma=\mathscr D_{\theta(q)}$.
		  
	Since $\sigma\in\mathcal D$, Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(3) gives
	$h_*\sigma\ll\sigma$ and $\sigma\ll h_*\sigma$ for every $h\in\mathsf{H}$.
	Since $\sigma=\delta_{\theta(q)}$, we have
	$h_*\sigma=\delta_{h\theta(q)}$. Thus, the mutual absolute continuity
	of $\delta_{h\theta(q)}$ and $\delta_{\theta(q)}$ forces
	$h\theta(q)=\theta(q)$. The equivariance and injectivity of $\theta$
	then give $hq=q$ for every $h\in\mathsf{H}$, contradicting
\cite[Prop.~12.1(1), Thm.~12.2(1)]{KB}. This proves the lemma.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:shadowprinciple}
	Let $(\mathsf{H},\chi)$ be as above and let $\delta>0$. For every
	$R\geq R_2$, there exists
	$C_R=C_R(\varphi,\chi,\delta,\mathsf{H})\geq1$ such that, for every
	$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ and
	$\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
	\[
	C_R^{-1}\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
	e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
	\leq
	\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
	\leq
	C_R\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
	e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}.
	\]
	In particular, if $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, then for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$,
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:shadow-character}
		C_R^{-1}e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq
		\frac{\nu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\bigr)}
		{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}
		\leq
		C_Re^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(2),
	\[
	\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
	=
	\int_{\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
	e^{-\delta\varphi(
		\beta_\xi(\gamma_1o,\gamma_2o))}
	\,d\nu_{\gamma_2o}(\xi).
	\]
	For every $\xi\in\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$,
	Proposition~\ref{Prop:busctrl} gives
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		e^{-\delta A_R}e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
		\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
		&\leq
		\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)\\
		&\leq
		e^{\delta A_R}e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
		\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr).
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:fullness},
	\[
	\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
	\geq m_0\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert,
	\]
	while
	\[
	\nu_{\gamma_2o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
	\leq\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert.
	\]
	It follows that
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		m_0e^{-\delta A_R}\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
		&\leq
		\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)\\
		&\leq
		e^{\delta A_R}\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}\le m_0^{-1}e^{\delta A_R}\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Thus the first assertion follows with $	C_R:=m_0^{-1}e^{\delta A_R}.$
	If $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, applying the first assertion with
	$\gamma_1=e$, $\gamma_2=\gamma$ and using
	$\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	=e^{\chi(\gamma)}\lVert\nu_o\rVert$ from
	Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(1) gives
	\eqref{eq:shadow-character}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:shadow-consequences}
		Under the assumptions of
		\textup{Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple}}, fix
		$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$ and $R\geq R_2$.
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item 
			If $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, then
			\[
			e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			\leq C_R
			\qquad (\gamma\in\Gamma).
			\]
			
			\item
			For every $m\in\mathbb{R}$,
			\[
			\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
					m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1}}
			\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
			e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			\leq
			C_RM_R\lVert\nu_o\rVert,
			\]
			where $M_R$ is given by
			Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplicity}. In particular, if
			$\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, then
			\[
			\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
					m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1}}
			e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			\leq C_RM_R.
			\]
			
			\item 
			For every $\gamma\in\Gamma$,
			\[
			C_R^{-1}e^{-\delta d_{\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}}(e,\gamma)}
			\leq
			\frac{\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}
			{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}
			\leq
			C_Re^{\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}.
			\]
			
			\item 
			For every $\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$,
			\[
			\nu_{\gamma_1o}
			\bigl(\mathcal O_{6R}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
			\leq
			C_{6R}C_R\,
			\nu_{\gamma_1o}
			\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr).
			\]
			
			\item 
			\[
			\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
			\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
			e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			=+\infty.
			\]
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
For \textup{(1)}, the lower bound in
		\eqref{eq:shadow-character} and the inequality
		$\nu_o(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))\leq\lVert\nu_o\rVert$ give $e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq C_R$.
		
		For \textup{(2)}, Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple}, applied with
		$\gamma_1=e,\gamma=\gamma$  gives $\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq
		C_R\nu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\bigr).$
		Summing over all $\gamma\in\Gamma$ satisfying
		$m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1$ and applying
		Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplicity}, we obtain
		\[
		\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
				m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1}}
		\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq
		C_RM_R\lVert\nu_o\rVert.
		\]
		If $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, then
		$\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
		=e^{\chi(\gamma)}\lVert\nu_o\rVert$ by
		Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(1), which gives the particular case.
		
		For the lower bound in \textup{(3)}, apply
		Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple} with
		$\gamma_1=\gamma,\gamma_2=e$. Since
		$\nu_{\gamma o}(\mathcal O_R(\gamma,e))
		\leq\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert$, we obtain
		\[
		\frac{\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}
		{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}
		\geq
		C_R^{-1}e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma,e)}
		=
		C_R^{-1}e^{-\delta d_{\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}}(e,\gamma)},
		\]
		where the equality follows from \eqref{eq:dphi-flip}.
		Applying the theorem with
		$\gamma_1=e,\gamma_2=\gamma$ and using
		$\nu_o(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))\leq\lVert\nu_o\rVert$ similarly gives
		\[
		\frac{\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}
		{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}
		\leq
		C_Re^{\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}.
		\]
		
		For \textup{(4)}, applying
		Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple} at the radii $6R$ and $R$,
		respectively, gives
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_{6R}(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr)
			\leq
			C_{6R}\lVert\nu_{\gamma_2o}\rVert
			e^{-\deltad_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)}
			\leq
			C_{6R}C_R
			\nu_{\gamma_1o}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)\bigr).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		
	For (5), let $(\gamma_k)_{k\geq1}$ be an enumeration of $\Gamma$.
	For every $x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$, choose a geodesic ray $c$ from
	$e$ to $x$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow-basic}(2), we have 
	$x\in\mathcal O_R(e,c(n))$ for every $n\geq0$. 
	     
	Since the elements $c(n)$ are pairwise distinct, our identification
	of $\partial_\infty\Gamma$ with $\Lambda_\Gamma$ gives
	$\Lambda_\Gamma
	\subseteq
	\limsup_{k\to\infty}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_k).$ 
	If
$\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
\nu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\bigr)<\infty,$
	then  the Borel--Cantelli gives $\nu_o\left(
	\limsup_{k\to\infty}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_k)
	\right)=0,$
	contradicting
	$\nu_o(\Lambda_\Gamma)=\lVert\nu_o\rVert>0$. Therefore, we have 
	\[
	\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
	\nu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\bigr)=+\infty.
	\]
	Finally, Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple} gives
	$\nu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\bigr)
	\leq
	C_R\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)},$
	and hence $	\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
	\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
	=+\infty.$
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- dfn-8: body_md -->
\label{def:twisted-critical-exponent}
	For $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$, define
	\[
	Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
	:=
	\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)-s\,d_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
\qquad
	\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)
	:=
	\limsup_{m\to\infty}
	\frac{1}{m}
	\log
	\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
			m-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m}}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)}.
	\]
	We write
	$\delta_\varphi(\Gamma):=\delta_{\varphi,0}(\Gamma)$. For a
	subgroup $\mathsf{H}\leq\Gamma$ and
	$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{H},\mathbb{R})$, the quantities
	$Q_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi}(s)$,
	$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\mathsf{H})$, and
	$\delta_\varphi(\mathsf{H})$ are defined analogously by summing over
	$h\in\mathsf{H}$.
<!-- end dfn-8 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:abscissa}
	Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$. Then
	$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)<\infty$ whenever
	$s>\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$, and
	$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)=+\infty$ whenever
	$s<\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$. The same assertions hold for
	every subgroup $\mathsf{H}\leq\Gamma$ and every
	$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{H},\mathbb{R})$. Moreover, for fixed
	$\gamma_1,\gamma_2\in\Gamma$, the value of
	$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$ is unchanged if
	$d_\varphi(e,\gamma)$ is replaced by
	$d_\varphi(\gamma_1,\gamma\gamma_2)$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Write
	\[
	S_m
	:=
	\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
			m-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m}}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)}
	\qquad\text{and}\qquad
	\delta
	:=
	\limsup_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\log S_m.
	\]
	If $m-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m$, then
	$	e^{-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
	\in
	\left[
	e^{-|s|}e^{-sm},
	e^{|s|}e^{-sm}
	\right].$
	Suppose first that $s>\delta$. Choose
	$\varepsilon\in(0,s-\delta)$. By the definition of the limsup,
	there exists $M\in\mathbb{N}$ such that
	$S_m\leq e^{(\delta+\varepsilon)m}$ for every $m\geq M.$
	The preceding estimate gives
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\sum_{m\geq M}
		\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
				m-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m}}
		e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq
		e^{|s|}
		\sum_{m\geq M}S_me^{-sm}
		\leq
		e^{|s|}
		\sum_{m\geq M}
		e^{-(s-\delta-\varepsilon)m}
		<\infty.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Moreover, Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(1) gives
	\[
	\{\gamma\in\Gamma:d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq M-1\}
	\subseteq
	B_w\bigl(e,A(M+B-1)\bigr).
	\]
	Since $\Gamma$ is finitely generated, the ball on the right is
	finite. Therefore,
	\[
	\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
			d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq M-1}}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
	<\infty.
	\]
	Combining the last two estimates yields
	$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)<\infty$. Now suppose that $s<\delta$. Choose
	$\varepsilon>0$ such that $\varepsilon \in (0, \delta-s)$. There exists a
	sequence $m_k\to\infty$ for which $S_{m_k}\geq e^{(\delta-\varepsilon)m_k}.$ Hence, we obtain
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
				m_k-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m_k}}
		e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\geq
		e^{-|s|}S_{m_k}e^{-sm_k}
		\geq
		e^{-|s|}
		e^{(\delta-\varepsilon-s)m_k}
		\longrightarrow+\infty.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Since all terms of $Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)$ are non-negative,
	\[
	Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
	\geq
	\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
			m_k-1<d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\leq m_k}}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
	\]
	for every $k$. Letting $k\to\infty$ gives
	$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)=+\infty$.
	The same argument applies verbatim to every subgroup
	$\mathsf{H}\leq\Gamma$, with $\gamma$ replaced by $h\in\mathsf{H}$. 
	
	The last assertion follows from
	Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(2), since the two distances differ by a bounded amount.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:delta-props}
	Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ and
	$\varphi\in\mathcal L^{>0}_\Gamma$.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item We have $\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}\in\mathcal L^{>0}_\Gamma$ and
		$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi^{\mathrm{i}},-\chi}(s)
		=Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)$ for every $s\in\mathbb{R}$. Consequently,
		$\delta_{\varphi^{\mathrm{i}},-\chi}(\Gamma)
		=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$, and
		$(\Gamma,\varphi^{\mathrm{i}},-\chi)$ is of divergence type if and only
		if $(\Gamma,\varphi,\chi)$ is.
		
		\item We have $0<\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)<+\infty$ and
		\[
		\left|
		\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)-\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)
		\right|
		\leq c_\chi A,
		\]
		where $c_\chi:=\max_{s\in S}\lvert\chi(s)\rvert$ and $A$ is the
		constant in Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(1).
		
		\item If $\mathsf{H}\leq\Gamma$, then
		$\delta_{\varphi,\chi|_{\mathsf{H}}}(\mathsf{H})
		\leq\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$.
		
		\item If $\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}=\varphi$, then
		$\delta_{\varphi,-\chi}(\Gamma)
		=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)
		\geq\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
(1) By \eqref{eq:kappa-inverse},
		$\mathrm{i}(\mathcal L_\Gamma)=\mathcal L_\Gamma$, and hence
		$\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}\in\mathcal L^{>0}_\Gamma$. Moreover,
		\[
		d_{\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}}(e,\gamma^{-1})
		=
		\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}\bigl(\kappa(\gamma^{-1})\bigr)
		=
		\varphi\bigl(\mathrm{i}^2\kappa(\gamma)\bigr)
		=
		d_\varphi(e,\gamma).
		\]
		Since 
		$\chi(\gamma^{-1})=-\chi(\gamma)$, reindexing the series gives
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi^{\mathrm{i}},-\chi}(s)
			&=
			\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
			e^{-\chi(\gamma^{-1})
				-sd_{\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}}(e,\gamma^{-1})}\\
			&=
			\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
			e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			=
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		The remaining assertions follow immediately.
		
		(2) Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(1) gives $d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\geq A^{-1}|\gamma|-B.$
		Therefore, for $s>A\log(|S|)$,
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,0}(s)
			&\leq
			e^{sB}
			\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}
			\left|\{\gamma\in\Gamma:|\gamma|=n\}\right|
			e^{-sn/A}\\
			&\leq
			e^{sB}
			\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}
			|S|^n e^{-sn/A}
			<\infty.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Hence $Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,0}(s)<\infty$, and
		Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa} gives
		$\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)\leq s<+\infty$.
		       
		Since $\chi$ is a homomorphism, we have 
		$	|\chi(\gamma)|
		\leq c_\chi|\gamma|
		\leq c_\chi A\bigl(d_\varphi(e,\gamma)+B\bigr).$
		It follows that
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			e^{-c_\chi AB}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,0}(s+c_\chi A)
			\leq
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
			\leq
			e^{c_\chi AB}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,0}(s-c_\chi A).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa} now gives
		\[
		\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)-c_\chi A
		\leq
		\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)
		\leq
		\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)+c_\chi A.
		\]
		In particular,
		$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)<+\infty$.
		        
		It remains to establish positivity. Let $\mathsf N:=[\Gamma,\Gamma]$.
		Since $\Gamma$ is non-elementary, \cite[Thm.~12.2(1)]{KB} gives a
		free subgroup $\mathsf F_2\leq\Gamma$ of rank two.  Since
		$[\mathsf F_2,\mathsf F_2]\leq\mathsf N$ is infinite,
		$\mathsf N$ is infinite. As $\mathsf N\lhd\Gamma$,
		\cite[Thm.~12.2(5)]{KB} gives
		$\Lambda(\mathsf N)=\Lambda(\Gamma)=\partial_\infty\Gamma$. In particular,
		$\mathsf N$ is non-elementary, so applying
		\cite[Thm.~12.2(1)]{KB} to $\mathsf N\leq\Gamma$ yields a
		free subgroup $\mathsf F:=\langle a,b\rangle\leq\mathsf N$ of rank two.
		        
		Set $L:=\max\{|a|,|b|\}$. For each $n\geq1$, let
		\[
		W_n
		:=
		\left\{
		s_1\cdots s_n:
		s_j\in\{a,b\}
		\right\}.
		\]
		The sets $W_n$ are pairwise disjoint and
		$\lvert W_n\rvert=2^n$. Since $W_n\subseteq\mathsf N\subseteq\ker\chi$, we have
		$\chi(w)=0$ for every $w\in W_n$. Moreover,
		Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(1), together with $|w|\leq nL$, gives
		\[
		d_\varphi(e,w)
		\leq A|w|+B
		\leq ALn+B.
		\]
		Consequently, for
		$0<s<	\frac{\log 2}{AL}$,
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
			&\geq
			\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}
			\sum_{w\in W_n}
			e^{-sd_\varphi(e,w)}\\
			&\geq
			e^{-sB}
			\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}
			\left(2e^{-sAL}\right)^n
			=
			+\infty.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Therefore, Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa} yields
		$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)
		\geq
		\frac{\log 2}{AL}
		>0.$
		
	(3) Since
	$Q_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi|_{\mathsf{H}}}(s)\leq
	Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)$ for every $s\in\mathbb{R}$, the assertion
	follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa}.
		
		(4) If $\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}=\varphi$, then (1) gives $\delta_{\varphi,-\chi}(\Gamma)
		=
		\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma).$
		On the other hand,
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
			+
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,-\chi}(s)
			=
			\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
			\left(e^{\chi(\gamma)}+e^{-\chi(\gamma)}\right)
			e^{-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			\geq
			2Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,0}(s).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa}, this yields
	$\max\left\{
	\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma),
	\delta_{\varphi,-\chi}(\Gamma)
	\right\}
	\geq\delta_\varphi(\Gamma).$
	Together with the preceding equality, this proves (4).
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:rigidity}
		Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ and $\delta>0$. If
		$\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\Gamma)\neq\emptyset$, then
		$\delta=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$ and
		$Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}
		\bigl(\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)\bigr)=+\infty$.
		In particular, $(\Gamma,\varphi,\chi)$ is of divergence type.
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Fix $R=R_2$ and
		$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\Gamma)$. By
		Corollary~\ref{cor:shadow-consequences}(2) and
		Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(1), for every $m\in\mathbb{Z}$,
		\[
		\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
				m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1}}
		e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		\leq C_RM_R.
		\]
		By Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(1), choose $m_-\in\mathbb{Z}$ such that
		$d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\geq m_-$ for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$.
		For $s>\delta$, summing over the annuli gives
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
			&=
			\sum_{m\geq m_-}
			\sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\
					m\leqd_\varphi(e,\gamma)<m+1}}
			e^{\chi(\gamma)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
			e^{-(s-\delta)d_\varphi(e,\gamma)}\\
			&\leq
			C_RM_R\sum_{m\geq m_-}e^{-(s-\delta)m}
			<\infty.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
	Hence 
	Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa} implies that
	$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)\leq\delta$. On the other hand,
		Corollary~\ref{cor:shadow-consequences}(5) and
		Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(1) give
		\[
		Q_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(\delta)
		=
		\frac{1}{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}
		\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
		\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
		e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		=+\infty.
 		\]
		Lemma~\ref{lem:abscissa} therefore gives
		$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)\geq\delta$. Together with the opposite
		inequality, this shows that $\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)=\delta$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:poincare}
		Let $\mathsf{H}\lhd\Gamma$ be a non-elementary normal subgroup and let
		$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{H},\mathbb{R})$ satisfy \eqref{eq:conj-inv}. If
		$\delta>0$ and $\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$, then
		\[
		\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
		\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert\,
		e^{-s\,d_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
		<\infty
		\qquad\text{for every }s>\delta .
		\]
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
The assertion follows from Corollary~\ref{cor:shadow-consequences}(2), as in the first
		part of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:rigidity}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:patterson-h}
	Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ and set
	$\delta:=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$. There exists a
	non-decreasing function $f\colon\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow(0,\infty)$
	such that, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there is
	$T_\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}$ with
	$f(t+r)\leq e^{\varepsilon r}f(t)$
for all $t\geq T_\varepsilon,\ r\geq0)$.
	Moreover, the modified series
	\[
	Q^f_{\Gamma,\varphi,\chi}(s)
	:=
	\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
	e^{\chi(\gamma)-sd_\varphi(e,\gamma)}
	f\bigl(d_\varphi(e,\gamma)\bigr)
	\]
	converges for $s>\delta$ and diverges for $s\leq\delta$.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Apply Patterson's construction \cite[Lem.~3.1]{Patterson},
	as in \cite[proof of Prop.~11.10(i)]{PPS}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:existence}
	Let $\mathsf{H}\leq\Gamma$ be a subgroup and
	$\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathsf{H},\mathbb{R})$. Suppose that
	$\delta:=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\mathsf{H})$ is finite. Then there
	exists a family $\mu=(\mu_x)_{x\in\mathbb X}$ of finite
	non-zero Borel measures on $\mathcal F$, supported on $\Lambda_\Gamma$, that
	satisfies \eqref{eq:conf} with dimension $\delta$ and
	\eqref{eq:equiv} for every element of $\mathsf{H}$.
	  
	If $\mathsf{H}$ is an infinite normal subgroup of $\Gamma$, then
	$\operatorname{supp}\mu_x=\Lambda_\Gamma$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$. If, in addition,
	$\chi$ satisfies \eqref{eq:conj-inv}, then
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$. In particular,
	$\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}
	(\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma),\Gamma)\neq\emptyset$
	for every $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$.
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Let $f$ and $Q^f_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi}$ be as in
		Lemma~\ref{lem:patterson-h}. For $s>\delta$, define a probability
		measure on $\overline\Gamma$ by
		\[
		\widetilde\mu_s
		:=
		\frac{1}{Q^f_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi}(s)}
		\sum_{h\in\mathsf{H}}
		e^{\chi(h)-sd_\varphi(e,h)}
		f\bigl(d_\varphi(e,h)\bigr)\mathscr D_h.
		\]
		Since $\overline\Gamma$ is compact, we may choose
		$s_k\downarrow\delta$ such that
		$\widetilde\mu_{s_k}\to\widetilde\mu$ weak-$\ast$ for some
		$\widetilde\mu\in\mathcal P(\overline\Gamma)$.
		Lemma~\ref{lem:patterson-h} and Fatou's lemma give
		$Q^f_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi}(s_k)\to+\infty$.
		Consequently,
		$\widetilde\mu_{s_k}(\{\gamma\})\to0$ for every
		$\gamma\in\Gamma$. Each point of $\Gamma$ is isolated in
		$\overline\Gamma$, so weak-$\ast$ convergence gives
		$\widetilde\mu(\{\gamma\})=0$. As $\Gamma$ is countable,
		$\widetilde\mu(\Gamma)=0$. Thus $\widetilde\mu$ is a probability
		measure on $\partial_\infty\Gamma$.
		  
		Set
		\[
		\mu_o:=\theta_\ast\widetilde\mu,
		\qquad
		\mu_x:=e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,o))}\mu_o
		\quad(x\in\mathbb X).
		\]
		By Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(4), these are finite non-zero
		Borel measures supported on $\Lambda_\Gamma$. The cocycle identity in
		Lemma~\ref{lem:beta-props}(1) gives
		$\mu_x=e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}\mu_y$.
		Hence $\mu$ satisfies \eqref{eq:conf}.
		
		It remains to prove twisted equivariance. Fix $\gamma\in\mathsf{H}$.
		Since $\gamma_\ast\mathscr D_h=\mathscr D_{\gamma h}$,
		reindexing by $\beta=\gamma h$ gives
		\[
		e^{\chi(\gamma)}\gamma_\ast\widetilde\mu_s
		=
	R_s\widetilde{\mu}_s
		\]
		where, for $\beta\in\mathsf{H}$,
		\[
		R_s(\beta)
		:=
		e^{-s[d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta)-d_\varphi(e,\beta)]}
		\frac{f\bigl(d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta)\bigr)}
		{f\bigl(d_\varphi(e,\beta)\bigr)}.
		\]
		Set $L:=\sqrt d\,\lVert\varphi\rVert\,
		\lVert\kappa(\gamma)\rVert$.
		By Lemma~\ref{lem:dphi-qi}(2),
		\[
		\bigl|d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta)-d_\varphi(e,\beta)\bigr|\leq L.
		\]
		For $\varepsilon>0$, let $T_\varepsilon$ be as in
		Lemma~\ref{lem:patterson-h}. If
		$d_\varphi(e,\beta)\geq T_\varepsilon+L$, then both
		$d_\varphi(e,\beta)$ and $d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta)$ are at least
		$T_\varepsilon$. Therefore, we have $f(d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta))/f(d_\varphi(e,\beta))
		\in[e^{-\varepsilon L},e^{\varepsilon L}]$.
		By the Anosov hypothesis, there exist $c>0$ and $C\geq0$ such
		that
		\[
		\min_{1\leq i\leq d-1}\alpha_i(\kappa(\beta))
		\geq c\,d_w(e,\beta)-C
		\qquad(\beta\in\mathsf{H}).
		\]
		Therefore, the Anosov estimate  and  Lemma~\ref{lem:sigma-props}(4)  gives
		\[
		d_\varphi(\gamma,\beta)-d_\varphi(e,\beta)
		-\varphi\bigl(\sigma(\gamma^{-1},U(\beta))\bigr)
		\longrightarrow0
		\qquad(d_w(e,\beta)\to\infty).
		\]
		Define $V_\gamma\colon\overline\Gamma\to(0,\infty)$ by
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			V_\gamma(\beta)
			&:=e^{-\delta\varphi(\sigma(\gamma^{-1},U(\beta)))},
			\qquad (\beta\in\Gamma),\\
			V_\gamma(x)
			&:=e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_{\theta(p)}(\gamma o,o))},
			\qquad (x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma).
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Note that $V_\gamma \in \mathcal{C}(\overline{\Gamma})$.
			Combining the preceding estimates, for every $\varepsilon>0$
		there is a finite set $E_\varepsilon\subset\mathsf{H}$, independent of
		$k$, such that, for $\beta\in\mathsf{H}\smallsetminus E_\varepsilon$,
		\[
		R_{s_k}(\beta)\in
		V_\gamma(\beta)
		\left[
		e^{-((|\delta|+L)\varepsilon+(s_k-\delta)L)},
		e^{((|\delta|+L)\varepsilon+(s_k-\delta)L)}
		\right].
		\]
		  
	Let $0\leq F\in \mathcal C(\overline\Gamma)$.
	Multiply the preceding inequalities by $F$ and integrate over
	$\mathsf{H}\setminus E_\varepsilon$ with respect to
	$\widetilde\mu_{s_k}$.
	The contributions from $E_\varepsilon$ tend to zero, since their
	unnormalised weights remain bounded while
	$Q^f_{\mathsf{H},\varphi,\chi}(s_k)\to+\infty$.
	By weak-$\ast$ convergence and the continuity of $V_\gamma$,
	letting $k\to\infty$ and then $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ yields
	\[
	e^{\chi(\gamma)}\gamma_\ast\widetilde\mu
	=V_\gamma\,\widetilde\mu.
	\]
	Since $\theta$ is $\Gamma$-equivariant, pushing forward the previous identity gives
	$e^{\chi(\gamma)}\gamma_\ast\mu_o=\mu_{\gamma o}$. By conformality and the
	$\Gamma$-equivariance of $\beta$, we then get
	$\gamma_\ast\mu_x=e^{-\chi(\gamma)}\mu_{\gamma x}$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$.
	Thus \eqref{eq:equiv} holds.
	
	If $\mathsf{H}$ is infinite and normal in $\Gamma$, then
	$\theta^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}\mu_o)$ is a non-empty closed
	$\mathsf{H}$-invariant subset of $\partial_\infty\Gamma$. Hence it equals $\partial_\infty\Gamma$ by
	\cite[Prop.~12.1(1), Thm.~12.2(5)]{KB}.
	Thus $\operatorname{supp}\mu_x=\Lambda_\Gamma$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$, and
	$\delta>0$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-props}(2). If
	\eqref{eq:conj-inv} holds, then
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
	
	For $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-props}(2)
	gives $\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)<\infty$, proving the final claim.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- cor-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:jordan-constraint}
		Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ and $\delta:=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$. Then
		for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$,
		\[
		-\,\delta\,\varphi\big(\mathrm{i}\,\lambda(\gamma)\big)\ \leq\ \chi(\gamma)\ \leq\
		\delta\,\varphi\big(\lambda(\gamma)\big);
		\]
		in particular, if $\varphi$ is symmetric,
		$\lvert\chi(\gamma)\rvert\leq\delta\,\varphi(\lambda(\gamma))$.
<!-- end cor-4 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
By Theorem~\ref{thm:existence} and
		Corollary~\ref{cor:shadow-consequences}(1), there exists
		$C\geq1$ such that
		$\chi(\gamma')\leq\delta\,d_\varphi(e,\gamma')+\log C$
		for every $\gamma'\in\Gamma$.
		Hence, for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$ and $n\geq1$,
		\[
		\chi(\gamma)
		=
		\frac{\chi(\gamma^n)}{n}
		\leq
		\delta\,\varphi\!\left(\frac{\kappa(\gamma^n)}{n}\right)
		+\frac{\log C}{n}.
		\]
		Using $\kappa(\gamma^n)/n\to\lambda(\gamma)$ and letting
		$n\to\infty$ gives
		$\chi(\gamma)\leq\delta\,\varphi(\lambda(\gamma))$.
		Applying this bound to $\gamma^{-1}$, together with
		$\chi(\gamma^{-1})=-\chi(\gamma)$ and
		$\lambda(\gamma^{-1})=\mathrm{i}\lambda(\gamma)$, yields
		$\chi(\gamma)\geq-\delta\,\varphi(\mathrm{i}\lambda(\gamma))$.
		The final assertion follows when $\varphi\circ\mathrm{i}=\varphi$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:maximal}
	Let $\nu$ be a finite Borel measure on $\partial_\infty\Gamma$ and $\lambda>0$. Set
	\[
	I:=\{\gamma\in\Gamma:
	\nu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))>\lambda\,\mu(\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma))\}.
	\]
	Then
	\[
	\mu\left(\bigcup_{\gamma\in I}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\right)
	\leq\frac{\lVert\nu\rVert}{\lambda}.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
The set $E:=\bigcup_{\gamma\in I}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)$ is Borel,
	since $\Gamma$ is countable and shadows are closed.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:vitali}, there exists $J\subseteq I$ such that
	the shadows $\{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\}_{\gamma\in J}$ are pairwise disjoint
	and $E\subseteq\bigcup_{\gamma\in J}\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma)$. Hence
	\[
	\mu(E)
	\leq\sum_{\gamma\in J}\mu(\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma))
	\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}
	\sum_{\gamma\in J}\nu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))
	\leq\frac{\lVert\nu\rVert}{\lambda},
	\]
	where the last inequality follows from disjointness.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:differentiation}
	Let $\mu$ be as above and $f\in L^1(\mu)$. Then, for $\mu$-almost
	every $x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma$ and every sequence $(\gamma_i)$ converging radially
	to $x$,
	\[
	\lim_{i\to\infty}
	\frac{1}{\mu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i))}
	\int_{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i)}f\,d\mu=f(x).
	\]
	In particular, for every Borel set $A\subseteq\partial_\infty\Gamma$, the ratios
	$\mu(A\cap\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i))/\mu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i))$ converge to
	$\mathbb{1}_A(x)$ for $\mu$-almost every $x$ and every radial sequence
	converging to $x$.
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Choose an everywhere finite Borel representative of $f$ and set
	\[
	A_\gamma h
	:=
	\frac{1}{\mu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))}
	\int_{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)}h\,d\mu
	\qquad(h\in L^1(\mu),\ \gamma\in\Gamma).
	\]
	For every $g\in\mathcal C(\partial_\infty\Gamma)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:shrinking}
	and uniform continuity give
	\[
	\sup_{x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)}|A_\gamma g-g(x)|
	\leq
	\sup_{x,q\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)}|g(q)-g(x)|
	\longrightarrow0
	\qquad(|\gamma|\to\infty).
	\]
	
	For $t>0$, define
	\[
	E_t
	:=
	\left\{
	x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma:
	\lim_{N\to\infty}
	\sup_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma,\ |\gamma|\geq N\\
			x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)}}
	|A_\gamma f-f(x)|>t
	\right\}.
	\]
	Each $E_t$ is Borel, since $f$ is Borel, $\Gamma$ is countable,
	and shadows are Borel.
	
	Fix $g\in\mathcal C(\partial_\infty\Gamma)$ and put $h=f-g$. For
	$x\in\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)$,
	\[
	|A_\gamma f-f(x)|
	\leq A_\gamma|h|+|A_\gamma g-g(x)|+|h(x)|.
	\]
	Together with the preceding convergence, this implies
	\[
	E_t
	\subseteq
	\{x\in\partial_\infty\Gamma:|h(x)|>t/2\}
	\cup
	\bigcup_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma\\ A_\gamma|h|>t/2}}
	\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma).
	\]
	Whenever $A_\gamma|h|>t/2$, \eqref{eq:doubling} gives
	\[
	\int_{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)}|h|\,d\mu
	>
	\frac{t}{2}\mu(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))
	\geq
	\frac{t}{2D}\mu(\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma)).
	\]
	Thus Lemma~\ref{lem:maximal}, applied to
	$\nu=|h|\mu$ and $\lambda=t/(2D)$, together with
	Markov's inequality, yields
	\[
	\mu(E_t)
	\leq
	\frac{2}{t}\lVert h\rVert_{L^1(\mu)}
	+
	\frac{2D}{t}\lVert h\rVert_{L^1(\mu)}
	=
	\frac{2(D+1)}{t}\lVert f-g\rVert_{L^1(\mu)}.
	\]
	Since $\mathcal C(\partial_\infty\Gamma)$ is dense in $L^1(\mu)$, it follows
	that $\mu(E_t)=0$ for every $t>0$.
	
	For every $x\notin\bigcup_{n\geq1}E_{1/n}$, the limit
	defining $E_t$ is zero. Hence $A_{\gamma_i}f\to f(x)$
	along every sequence $(\gamma_i)$ converging radially to $x$.
	The final assertion follows by taking $f=\mathbb{1}_A$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:masscomparison}
	Let $(\mathsf{H},\chi)$ be as in Section~\ref{sec:densities}.
	Fix $\delta>0$ and $R\geq R_2$, and let
	$\mu,\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$. Suppose that
	$\Theta\geq1$ satisfies
	\[
	\Theta^{-1}\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	\leq\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	\leq\Theta\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma).
	\]
	Then $\mu_o$ and $\nu_o$ are mutually absolutely continuous, with
	\[
	(\Theta C_RC_{6R})^{-1}
	\leq\frac{d\nu_o}{d\mu_o}
	\leq\Theta C_RC_{6R}
	\qquad\mu_o\text{-a.e.}
	\]
	The one-sided assumption
	$\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert\leq\Theta\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert$
	for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$ already implies
	$\nu_o\leq\Theta C_RC_{6R}\mu_o$.
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
It suffices to prove the one-sided assertion. Let $B\subseteq\partial_\infty\Gamma$
	be Borel and $\varepsilon>0$. By outer regularity, choose an open
	$V\supseteq B$ with $\mu_o(V)\leq\mu_o(B)+\varepsilon$, and set
	\[
	I:=\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma)\subseteq V\}.
	\]
	For each $x\in V$, choose a geodesic ray $c$ from $e$ to $x$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow-basic}(2),
	$x\in\mathcal O_R(e,c(n))\subseteq\mathcal O_{6R}(e,c(n))$.
	Lemma~\ref{lem:shrinking}, applied at radius $6R$, gives
	$\mathcal O_{6R}(e,c(n))\subseteq V$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
	Thus $V=\bigcup_{\gamma\in I}\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)$.
	        
	Since $R\geq R_2\geq R_1$, Lemma~\ref{lem:vitali} provides
	$J\subseteq I$ such that the shadows
	$\{\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\}_{\gamma\in J}$ are pairwise disjoint and
	\[
	B\subseteq\bigcup_{\gamma\in J}\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma)\subseteq V.
	\]
	Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:shadowprinciple} at radii $6R$ and $R$
	and using the mass bound, we obtain
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\nu_o(B)
		&\leq\sum_{\gamma\in J}\nu_o(\mathcal O_{6R}(e,\gamma))\\
		&\leq C_{6R}\sum_{\gamma\in J}
		\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert e^{-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma)}\\
		&\leq\Theta C_RC_{6R}
		\sum_{\gamma\in J}\mu_o(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma))\\
		&\leq\Theta C_RC_{6R}\mu_o(V),
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	where the last inequality uses disjointness and
	$\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma)\subseteq V$ for $\gamma\in J$.
	Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ gives
	$\nu_o\leq\Theta C_RC_{6R}\mu_o$.
	Interchanging $\mu$ and $\nu$ proves the reverse inequality and
	the stated Radon--Nikodym bounds.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:fatou}
	Let $(\mathsf{H},\chi)$ be as in Section~\ref{sec:densities}, let $\delta>0$,
	and let $\mu,\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item If
		$\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert\geq\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert$
		for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$, then $\mu_x\geq\nu_x$ as measures
		for every $x\in\mathbb X$.
		\item If
		$\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert=\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert$
		for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$, then $\mu=\nu$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Assume the hypothesis of \textup{(1)}. Fix $R\geq R_2$ and put
	$C^\ast:=C_RC_{6R}$.
	Proposition~\ref{prop:masscomparison} gives
	$\nu_o\leq C^\ast\mu_o$. Set
	\[
	\tau:=\sup\{t\geq0:\mu_o\geq t\nu_o\}.
	\]
	Then
	\[
	(C^\ast)^{-1}\leq\tau
	\leq\frac{\lVert\mu_o\rVert}{\lVert\nu_o\rVert}<\infty.
	\]
	Choose $t_n\uparrow\tau$ with $\mu_o\geq t_n\nu_o$.
	For every Borel set $B\subseteq\mathcal F$,
	\[
	\mu_o(B)\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}t_n\nu_o(B)
	=\tau\nu_o(B).
	\]
	Thus $\mu_o\geq\tau\nu_o$.
	
	Suppose that $\tau<1$ and define
	\[
	\mu'_x:=\frac{\mu_x-\tau\nu_x}{1-\tau}
	\qquad(x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	By \eqref{eq:conf}, for every Borel set $B\subseteq\mathcal F$,
	\[
	\mu'_x(B)
	=\frac{1}{1-\tau}
	\int_B e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(x,o))}
	\,d(\mu_o-\tau\nu_o)(\xi)\geq0.
	\]
	Moreover, for every $\gamma\in\Gamma$,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\lVert\mu'_{\gamma o}\rVert
		&=\frac{\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert
			-\tau\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}{1-\tau}\\
		&\geq
		\frac{(1-\tau)\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert}{1-\tau}
		=\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert>0.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	In particular, $\mu'_o\neq0$, and the preceding integral formula
	gives $\mu'_x\neq0$ for every $x\in\mathbb X$.
	These measures are finite and supported on $\Lambda_\Gamma$. For
	$x,y\in\mathbb X$ and $h\in\mathsf{H}$, we also have
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\mu'_x
		&=\frac{e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}}
		{1-\tau}(\mu_y-\tau\nu_y)
		=e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}\mu'_y,\\
		h_\ast\mu'_x
		&=\frac{h_\ast\mu_x-\tau h_\ast\nu_x}{1-\tau}
		=e^{-\chi(h)}
		\frac{\mu_{hx}-\tau\nu_{hx}}{1-\tau}
		=e^{-\chi(h)}\mu'_{hx}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Hence $\mu'\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
	                                                  
	Applying Proposition~\ref{prop:masscomparison} to $(\mu',\nu)$
	therefore gives $\nu_o\leq C^\ast\mu'_o$. Consequently,
	\[
	\mu_o
	=\tau\nu_o+(1-\tau)\mu'_o
	\geq\left(\tau+\frac{1-\tau}{C^\ast}\right)\nu_o.
	\]
	Since $\tau+(1-\tau)/C^\ast>\tau$, this contradicts the
	definition of $\tau$. Thus $\tau\geq1$, so $\mu_o\geq\nu_o$.
	For every $x\in\mathbb X$ and Borel set $B\subseteq\mathcal F$,
	\[
	\mu_x(B)-\nu_x(B)
	=\int_B e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(x,o))}
	\,d(\mu_o-\nu_o)(\xi)\geq0.
	\]
	This proves \textup{(1)}. Applying \textup{(1)} in both
	directions proves \textup{(2)}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:reversibility}
	Assume $\varphi$ is symmetric and let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ be such that
	$\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)=\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)$. Then $\chi=0$.
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Set $\delta:=\delta_\varphi(\Gamma)$. By
	Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-props}\textup{(4)},
	$\delta_{\varphi,-\chi}(\Gamma)
	=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)=\delta$.
	Theorem~\ref{thm:existence} provides
	\[
	\mu^\pm\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\pm\chi}(\delta,\Gamma),
	\qquad
	\mu^0\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\delta,\Gamma),
	\qquad
	\lVert\mu^\pm_o\rVert=\lVert\mu^0_o\rVert=1.
	\]
	    
	Put $\mathsf{H}:=\ker\chi$. Since $\mathsf{H}$ contains $[\Gamma,\Gamma]$,
	it is a non-elementary normal subgroup by the argument in
	Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-props}\textup{(2)}.
	Define $\nu_x:=\tfrac12(\mu^+_x+\mu^-_x)$.
	Conformality follows from \eqref{eq:conf}, and
	\eqref{eq:equiv} gives
	\[
	h_\ast\nu_x
	=\tfrac12\bigl(
	e^{-\chi(h)}\mu^+_{hx}
	+e^{\chi(h)}\mu^-_{hx}\bigr)
	=\nu_{hx}
	\qquad(h\in \mathsf{H},\ x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	Thus $\nu,\mu^0\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\delta,\mathsf{H})$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(1)},
	\[
	\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	=\frac{e^{\chi(\gamma)}+e^{-\chi(\gamma)}}{2}
	=\cosh\chi(\gamma)
	\geq1=\lVert\mu^0_{\gamma o}\rVert
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma).
	\]
	Theorem~\ref{thm:fatou}\textup{(1)} therefore yields
	$\nu_o\geq\mu^0_o$. Since both measures have total mass $1$,
	$\nu_o=\mu^0_o$, and \eqref{eq:conf} gives
	\[
	\nu_x-\mu^0_x
	=e^{-\delta\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,o))}
	(\nu_o-\mu^0_o)=0
	\qquad(x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	Consequently,
	\[
	\cosh\chi(\gamma)
	=\lVert\nu_{\gamma o}\rVert
	=\lVert\mu^0_{\gamma o}\rVert
	=1
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma),
	\]
	so $\chi=0$.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- dfn-9: body_md -->
\label{def:bms-measure}
	We define a Borel measure $\widetilde m$ on $\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma$ by
	\[
	d\widetilde m(\xi,\eta)
	:=e^{\delta\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}(\mathcal G(\xi,\eta))}
	\,d\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o(\xi)\,d\mu_o(\eta).
	\]
<!-- end dfn-9 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:bms-invariant}
	The measure $\widetilde m$ is a nonzero $\Gamma$-invariant Radon measure,
	and
	\[
	\widetilde m\ll
	(\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o\otimes\mu_o)|_{\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma}\ll
	\widetilde m.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Put
	\[
	\lambda:=(\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o\otimes\mu_o)|_{\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma},
	\qquad
	w(\xi,\eta):=e^{\delta\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}(\mathcal G(\xi,\eta))}.
	\]
	The measure $\lambda$ is finite, and $w$ is positive and continuous.
	Since $w$ is continuous and $K$ is compact, $\sup_K w<\infty$.
	Moreover, both $\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o$ and $\mu_o$ are finite measures, so
	\[
	\lambda(K)
	\leq(\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o\otimes\mu_o)(\Lambda_\Gamma\times\Lambda_\Gamma)
	=\lVert\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o\rVert\,\lVert\mu_o\rVert<\infty.
	\]
	Consequently,
	\[
