	\widetilde m(K)=\int_K w\,d\lambda
	\leq\bigl(\sup_K w\bigr)\lambda(K)<\infty.
	\]
	Thus $\widetilde m$ is locally finite and hence Radon.
	Since $w>0$, we also have $\widetilde m\ll\lambda$ and
	$\widetilde m\gg\lambda$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(4), both boundary measures have
	full support on $\Lambda_\Gamma$. Choose disjoint nonempty relatively open
	sets $U,V\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$. Then
	$\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o(U)\mu_o(V)>0$ and $U\times V\subseteq\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma$,
	so $\widetilde m\neq0$.
	
	Fix $\gamma\in\Gamma$. By \eqref{eq:equiv} and \eqref{eq:conf},
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		d(\gamma_\ast\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o)(\xi)
		&=e^{\chi(\gamma)-\delta\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}(\beta_\xi(\gamma o,o))}
		\,d\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o(\xi),\\
		d(\gamma_\ast\mu_o)(\eta)
		&=e^{-\chi(\gamma)-\delta\varphi(\beta_\eta(\gamma o,o))}
		\,d\mu_o(\eta).
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	                                             
By Lemma~\ref{lem:gromov-equiv}(2) and
Lemma 	\ref{lem:beta-props}, together with $\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}\circ\mathrm{i}=\varphi$,
	give
	\[
	w(\gamma^{-1}\xi,\gamma^{-1}\eta)
	=w(\xi,\eta)
	e^{\delta\varphi^{\mathrm{i}}(\beta_\xi(\gamma o,o))
		+\delta\varphi(\beta_\eta(\gamma o,o))}.
	\]
	Consequently, on $\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma$, we obtain
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		d(\gamma_\ast\widetilde m)(\xi,\eta)
		&=w(\gamma^{-1}\xi,\gamma^{-1}\eta)
		\,d(\gamma_\ast\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o)(\xi)\,d(\gamma_\ast\mu_o)(\eta)\\
		&=w(\xi,\eta)\,d\mu^{\mathrm{i}}_o(\xi)\,d\mu_o(\eta)\\
		&=d\widetilde m(\xi,\eta).
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Hence $\gamma_\ast\widetilde m=\widetilde m$, which proves $\Gamma$-invariance.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:HTS}
		Let $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$, $\delta=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$, and let
		$\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\Gamma)$,
		$\mu^\mathrm{i}\in\mathcal M_{\varphi^\mathrm{i},-\chi}(\delta,\Gamma)$. Then:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item the $\Gamma$-action on $(\Lambda^{(2)}_\Gamma,\widetilde m)$ is ergodic;
			\item the $\Gamma$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is ergodic, and likewise for
			$\mu^\mathrm{i}_o$;
			\item $\mu_o$ and $\mu^\mathrm{i}_o$ are atomless;
			\item the $\Gamma$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is conservative;
			\item Every twisted $(\varphi,\chi)$-conformal density for
			$\Gamma$ has dimension $\delta$ and is a positive scalar multiple
			of $\mu$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
(1) 
			By Lemma~\ref{lem:bms-invariant}, $\widetilde m$ is a nonzero
			$\Gamma$-invariant Radon measure on $\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}$ satisfying
			\[
			\widetilde m
			\ll (\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\otimes\mu_o)|_{\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}}
			\ll \widetilde m.
			\]
				The claim follows from \cite[Theorem~3.1]{CantrellTanaka}
			via the $\Gamma$-equivariant homeomorphism $\theta\times\theta$.
		
		
	(2) Let $A\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$ be $\Gamma$-invariant Borel with
	$\mu_o(A)>0$. Then
	$\widetilde A:=(\Lambda_\Gamma\times A)\cap\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}$
	is $\Gamma$-invariant.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(4), $\operatorname{supp}\mu_o^\mathrm{i}=\Lambda_\Gamma$.
	Since $\Gamma$ is non-elementary,
	$\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus\{\eta\}$ is a nonempty relatively open subset
	of $\Lambda_\Gamma$ for every $\eta\in\Lambda_\Gamma$, and hence
	$\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus\{\eta\})>0$.
	Thus, by the definition of $\widetilde m$ and positivity of
	the density,
	\[
	\widetilde m(\widetilde A)
	=
	\int_A
	\left(
	\int_{\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus\{\eta\}}
	e^{\delta\varphi^\mathrm{i}(\mathcal G(\xi,\eta))}
	\,d\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\xi)
	\right)d\mu_o(\eta)
	>0.
	\]
	By (1), we obtain 
	\[
	0
	=
	\widetilde m(\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}\smallsetminus\widetilde A)
	=
	\int_{\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus A}
	\left(
	\int_{\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus\{\eta\}}
	e^{\delta\varphi^\mathrm{i}(\mathcal G(\xi,\eta))}
	\,d\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\xi)
	\right)d\mu_o(\eta).
	\]
	Since the inner integral is strictly positive for every $\eta\in\Lambda_\Gamma$,
	we obtain $\mu_o(\Lambda_\Gamma\smallsetminus A)=0$.
	The same argument using the first factor and
	$\operatorname{supp}\mu_o=\Lambda_\Gamma$ proves ergodicity with respect to $\mu_o^\mathrm{i}$.
		
(3) Suppose that
$a:=\mu_o(\{\xi_0\})>0$, where $\xi_0=\theta(x_0)$.
Let $(\gamma_i)_{i\geq0}$ be the successive vertices of a
geodesic ray from $e$ to $x_0$, with $\gamma_0=e$.
Then $|\gamma_i|=i$.
Choose $R$ sufficiently large for the shadow estimates of both
densities, with a common constant $C\geq1$.
By Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow-basic}(2) and
\eqref{eq:shadow-character},
\[
a
\leq \mu_o\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i)\bigr)
\leq C e^{\chi(\gamma_i)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma_i)}
\lVert\mu_o\rVert.
\]
Moreover,  applying 
 \eqref{eq:shadow-character} to $\mu^\mathrm{i}$ with $-\chi(\gamma_i^{-1})=\chi(\gamma_i)$ and  $d_{\varphi^\mathrm{i}}(e,\gamma_i^{-1})=d_\varphi(e,\gamma_i)$ gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
	\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\bigl(\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i^{-1})\bigr)
	&\geq C^{-1}
	e^{-\chi(\gamma_i^{-1})
		-\delta d_{\varphi^\mathrm{i}}(e,\gamma_i^{-1})}
	\lVert\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\rVert\\
	&=C^{-1}e^{\chi(\gamma_i)-\deltad_\varphi(e,\gamma_i)}
	\lVert\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\rVert\\
	&\geq C^{-2}
	\frac{\lVert\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\rVert}{\lVert\mu_o\rVert}\,a
	=:b>0.
\end{aligned}
\]

Write $S_i:=\mathcal O_R(e,\gamma_i^{-1})$, endowing   $\Lambda_\Gamma$ with the visual metric $\rho$ via $\theta$
Since $|\gamma_i^{-1}|=i$, Lemma~\ref{lem:shrinking} gives
$\operatorname{diam}_\rho S_i\to0$.
Choose $\eta_i\in S_i$ and, by compactness, pass to a
subsequence such that $\eta_i\to\eta_0\in\Lambda_\Gamma$.
Then
\[
\sup_{\eta\in S_i}\rho(\eta,\eta_0)
\leq \operatorname{diam}_\rho S_i+\rho(\eta_i,\eta_0)
\longrightarrow0.
\]
Consequently, for every $\varepsilon>0$ and all sufficiently
large $i$,
\[
b\leq\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(S_i)
\leq\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\bigl(\overline B_\rho(\eta_0,\varepsilon)\bigr).
\]
Since $\mu_o^\mathrm{i}$ is finite, we obtain
\[
\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\{\eta_0\})
=\lim_{n\to\infty}
\mu_o^\mathrm{i}\bigl(\overline B_\rho(\eta_0,1/n)\bigr)
\geq b>0.
\]

                                                                      
By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(3), for every
$\gamma\in\Gamma$ and $\xi\in\Lambda_\Gamma$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
	\mu_o(\{\gamma\xi\})
	&=((\gamma^{-1})_\ast\mu_o)(\{\xi\})\\
	&=e^{\chi(\gamma)
		-\delta\varphi(\beta_\xi(\gamma^{-1}o,o))}
	\mu_o(\{\xi\}).
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $\Lambda_\Gamma$ is infinite and every $\Gamma$-orbit is dense
\cite[Theorem~2.28 and Proposition~4.2(2)]{KB},
choose $\gamma_0\in\Gamma$ such that
$\xi^\ast:=\gamma_0\xi_0\neq\xi':=\eta_0$.
Thus
$\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\{\xi'\})\mu_o(\{\xi^\ast\})>0$.
By Definition~\ref{def:bms-measure},
\[
\widetilde m\bigl(\{(\xi',\xi^\ast)\}\bigr)
=
e^{\delta\varphi^\mathrm{i}(\mathcal G(\xi',\xi^\ast))}
\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\{\xi'\})\mu_o(\{\xi^\ast\})
>0.
\]
The set $O:=\Gamma\cdot(\xi',\xi^\ast)$ is countable,
Borel and $\Gamma$-invariant. Hence (1) gives $\widetilde m(\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}\smallsetminus O)=0.$
    
Set
\[
\Pi':=\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\gamma\xi'=\xi'\},
\qquad
E:=\overline{\Pi'\xi^\ast}\cup\{\xi'\}.
\]
The subgroup $\Pi'$ fixes a boundary point, so it is finite
or virtually cyclic virtually cyclic (see \cite[Thm.~12.2(1)]{KB} and \cite[Ex.~2.4.3]{Calegari}).  Its boundary orbits therefore have at
most two accumulation points see \cite[Thm.~4.3]{KB}).
Thus $E$ is countable and closed.
Since $\Lambda_\Gamma$ is uncountable and
$\overline{\Gamma\xi^\ast}=\Lambda_\Gamma$, there exists
$\gamma_1\in\Gamma$ such that
$\gamma_1\xi^\ast\notin E$.
In particular, $\gamma_1\xi^\ast\neq\xi',$ and  $\mu_o(\{\gamma_1\xi^\ast\})>0.$
But $O\cap(\{\xi'\}\times\Lambda_\Gamma)
=\{\xi'\}\times(\Pi'\xi^\ast),$
so $(\xi',\gamma_1\xi^\ast)\notin O$.
Consequently,
\[
\begin{aligned}
	0
	&=\widetilde m(\Lambda_\Gamma^{(2)}\smallsetminus O)\\
	&\geq
	\widetilde m\bigl(\{(\xi',\gamma_1\xi^\ast)\}\bigr)\\
	&=
	e^{\delta\varphi^\mathrm{i}(\mathcal G(\xi',\gamma_1\xi^\ast))}
	\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\{\xi'\})\mu_o(\{\gamma_1\xi^\ast\})
	>0,
\end{aligned}
\]
a contradiction.
Therefore $\mu_o(\{\xi\})=0$ for every $\xi\in\Lambda_\Gamma$.
The shadow argument with the two densities interchanged
then gives $\mu_o^\mathrm{i}(\{\xi\})=0$ for every $\xi\in\Lambda_\Gamma$.



	(4) By (2), (3), and Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}(3), the action
	$\Gamma\curvearrowright(\mathcal F,\mu_o/\lVert\mu_o\rVert)$ is ergodic,
	non-singular, non-atomic, and free modulo $\mu_o$, since every nontrivial
	element fixes only two points of $\Lambda_\Gamma$. Hence it is conservative by
	\cite[Proposition~1.6.6]{Aaronson}.
		
	\textup{(5)} Let $\delta_1,\delta_2>0$ and
	$\mu^j\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta_j,\Gamma)$ for $j=1,2$.
	By Proposition~\ref{prop:rigidity},
	$\delta_1=\delta_2=\delta$.
	Set
	\[
	c:=\frac{\lVert\mu^1_o\rVert}{\lVert\mu^2_o\rVert}>0.
	\]
	Then $c\mu^2\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\delta,\Gamma)$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(1)}, for every
	$\gamma\in\Gamma$,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\lVert\mu^1_{\gamma o}\rVert
		&=e^{\chi(\gamma)}\lVert\mu^1_o\rVert\\
		&=c\,e^{\chi(\gamma)}\lVert\mu^2_o\rVert\\
		&=c\,\lVert\mu^2_{\gamma o}\rVert
		=\lVert(c\mu^2)_{\gamma o}\rVert.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:fatou}\textup{(2)} to
	$\mu^1$ and $c\mu^2$, with $\mathsf{H}=\Gamma$, gives
	$\mu^1=c\mu^2$.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:quasiinv}
		Let $1\leq k\leq n$, $\sigma>0$, $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$,
		and
		$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi|_{\Gamma^{(k)}}}
		(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)})$.
		For every $\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}$, there is
		$C_\gamma\geq1$, depending only on
		$(\gamma,\sigma,\varphi,\chi,\Gamma^{(k)})$, such that
		\[
		C_\gamma^{-1}\nu_o
		\leq(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma o}
		\leq C_\gamma\nu_o.
		\]
		In particular, $\gamma_\ast\nu_o\ll\nu_o$ and
		$\gamma_\ast\nu_o\gg\nu_o$ for every
		$\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}$.
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Fix $\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}$ and set
		$\nu^\gamma_x:=(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma x}$
		for $x\in\mathbb X$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:translates}
		and positive rescaling,
		\[
		\nu^\gamma\in
		\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi|_{\Gamma^{(k)}}}(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)}).
		\]
		For $\gamma'\in\Gamma$, put $c:=[\gamma,\gamma']
		=\gamma\gamma'\gamma^{-1}(\gamma')^{-1}
		\in\Gamma^{(k)}\cap\ker\chi.$
		Since $\gamma\gamma'=c\gamma'\gamma$,
		Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(1)} gives
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			\lVert\nu^\gamma_{\gamma'o}\rVert
			=\lVert\nu_{\gamma\gamma'o}\rVert
			=e^{\chi(c)}\lVert\nu_{\gamma'\gamma o}\rVert
			=\lVert\nu_{\gamma'\gamma o}\rVert.
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(5)},
		\[
		\left|
		\log\lVert\nu^\gamma_{\gamma'o}\rVert-\log \lVert\nu_{\gamma'o}\rVert
		\right|
		\leq
		\sigma\lVert\varphi\rVert
		\lVert\kappa(\gamma'o,\gamma'\gamma o)\rVert
		=
		\sigma\lVert\varphi\rVert\lVert\kappa(\gamma)\rVert.
		\]
		Thus, setting
		$\Theta_\gamma
		:=e^{\sigma\lVert\varphi\rVert\lVert\kappa(\gamma)\rVert}$,
		we have
		\[
		\Theta_\gamma^{-1}\lVert\nu_{\gamma'o}\rVert
		\leq\lVert\nu^\gamma_{\gamma'o}\rVert
		\leq\Theta_\gamma\lVert\nu_{\gamma'o}\rVert
		\qquad(\gamma'\in\Gamma).
		\]
		Fix $R\geq\max\{R_1,R_2\}$. Proposition~\ref{prop:masscomparison}
		therefore yields
		\[
		C_\gamma^{-1}\nu_o\leq\nu^\gamma_o\leq C_\gamma\nu_o,
		\qquad
		C_\gamma:=\Theta_\gamma C_RC_{6R}.
		\]
		Pushing forward by $\gamma$ and using \eqref{eq:conf}, we obtain
		\[
		C_\gamma^{-1}
		e^{-\sigma\varphi(\beta_\bullet(\gamma o,o))}\nu_o
		\leq\gamma_\ast\nu_o
		\leq
		C_\gamma
		e^{-\sigma\varphi(\beta_\bullet(\gamma o,o))}\nu_o.
		\]
		The weight is strictly positive, proving both
		$\gamma_\ast\nu_o\ll\nu_o$ and $\gamma_\ast\nu_o\gg\nu_o$.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:emerging}
	Let $\Gamma'\lhd\Gamma$ be non-elementary,
	$\Gamma'\leq S\leq\Gamma$, $\sigma>0$, and
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma')$.
	Suppose that the $\Gamma'$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is ergodic
	and the measure class of $\mu_o$ is $S$-invariant.
	Then there is a character $\chi'\in\mathrm{Hom}(S,\mathbb{R})$, vanishing
	on $\Gamma'$, such that
	\[
	\gamma_\ast\mu_x=e^{-\chi'(\gamma)}\mu_{\gamma x}
	\qquad(\gamma\in S,\ x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	Moreover,
	$\chi'(\gamma)=
	\log(\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert/\lVert\mu_o\rVert)$.
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
For $\gamma\in S$, set
	$\nu^\gamma_x:=(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\mu_{\gamma x}$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:translates} and positive rescaling,
	$\nu^\gamma\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma')$.
	Conformality and $S$-quasi-invariance give
	$\nu^\gamma_o\ll\mu_o$ and $\nu^\gamma_o\gg\mu_o$.
	Thus
	\[
	D_\gamma:=\frac{d\nu^\gamma_o}{d\mu_o}
	\quad\text{satisfies}\quad
	0<D_\gamma<\infty
	\qquad\mu_o\text{-a.e.}
	\]
	For $h\in\Gamma'$, put
	$J_h:=e^{-\sigma\varphi(\beta_\bullet(ho,o))}$.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(3)},
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		(D_\gamma\circ h^{-1})J_h\mu_o
		=h_\ast(D_\gamma\mu_o)
		=h_\ast\nu^\gamma_o
		=J_h\nu^\gamma_o
		=J_hD_\gamma\mu_o.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Since $J_h>0$, we have
	$D_\gamma\circ h^{-1}=D_\gamma$ $\mu_o$-a.e.
	Ergodicity therefore gives $D_\gamma=c(\gamma)$
	$\mu_o$-a.e., for some $c(\gamma)>0$.
	By \eqref{eq:conf},
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:translate-const}
		(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\mu_{\gamma x}
		=\nu^\gamma_x=c(\gamma)\mu_x
		\qquad(x\in\mathbb X).
	\end{equation}
	For $\gamma,\gamma'\in S$, this yields
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		c(\gamma\gamma')\mu_x
		&=((\gamma\gamma')^{-1})_\ast\mu_{\gamma\gamma'x}\\
		&=((\gamma')^{-1})_\ast
		\bigl((\gamma^{-1})_\ast\mu_{\gamma(\gamma'x)}\bigr)\\
		&=c(\gamma)c(\gamma')\mu_x.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Hence $c(\gamma\gamma')=c(\gamma)c(\gamma')$.
	Also, \eqref{eq:equiv} gives $c(h)=1$ for $h\in\Gamma'$.
	Thus $\chi':=\log c$ is a character vanishing on $\Gamma'$.
	Finally, \eqref{eq:translate-const} gives
	\[
	\gamma_\ast\mu_x=c(\gamma)^{-1}\mu_{\gamma x},
	\qquad
	c(\gamma)=
	\frac{\lVert\mu_{\gamma o}\rVert}{\lVert\mu_o\rVert},
	\]
	which proves both assertions.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:kill}
	Let $2\leq k\leq n$, $\sigma>0$, and
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)})$.
	Suppose that a character
	$\chi_{k-1}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma^{(k-1)},\mathbb{R})$ satisfies
	\[
	\gamma_\ast\mu_x
	=e^{-\chi_{k-1}(\gamma)}\mu_{\gamma x}
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)},\ x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	Then $\chi_{k-1}=0$, and hence
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k-1)})$.
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Since $\Gamma_0\subseteq\Gamma^{(k)}$, we have
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma_0)$.
	Choose a right-invariant mean $\mathfrak m$ on
	$\ell^\infty(\Gamma_0\backslash\Gamma)$ and set
	\[
	f_\gamma([\gamma'])
	:=\log\lVert\mu_{\gamma'\gamma o}\rVert
	-\log\lVert\mu_{\gamma'o}\rVert,
	\qquad
	\chi(\gamma):=\mathfrak m(f_\gamma)
	\qquad(\gamma,\gamma'\in\Gamma).
	\]
	
	For $\gamma_0\in\Gamma_0$ and $x\in\mathbb X$,
	equation~\eqref{eq:equiv} gives
	\[
	\lVert\mu^0_{\gamma_0x}\rVert
	=\lVert(\gamma_0)_\ast\mu^0_x\rVert
	=\lVert\mu^0_x\rVert.
	\]
	Hence $f_\gamma$ is well defined. By
	Lemma~\ref{lem:density-basic}\textup{(5)},
	\[
	|f_\gamma([\gamma'])|
	\leq\delta_0\lVert\varphi\rVert\,
	\lVert\kappa(\gamma'o,\gamma'\gamma o)\rVert
	=\delta_0\lVert\varphi\rVert\,
	\lVert\kappa(\gamma)\rVert.
	\]
	Since $f_{\gamma\gamma'}=f_\gamma+f_{\gamma'}\circ R_\gamma$,
	right-invariance of $\mathfrak m$ yields
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\chi_0(\gamma\gamma')
		=\mathfrak m(f_\gamma)
		+\mathfrak m(f_{\gamma'}\circ R_\gamma)
		=\chi_0(\gamma)+\chi_0(\gamma').
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Finally, normality gives
	$\gamma\gamma_0\gamma^{-1}\in\Gamma_0$, so
	\[
	f_{\gamma_0}([\gamma])
	=\log\frac{
		\lVert\mu^0_{(\gamma\gamma_0\gamma^{-1})\gamma o}\rVert}
	{\lVert\mu^0_{\gamma o}\rVert}
	=0.
	\]
	Thus $\chi_0(\gamma_0)=\mathfrak m(f_{\gamma_0})=0$.
	       
	This  shows that $f_\gamma$ is well
	defined and bounded, and that
	\[
	\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R}),
	\qquad
	\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0.
	\]
	
	Fix $\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}$ and $\gamma'\in\Gamma$.
	Since
	$\gamma\gamma'=[\gamma,\gamma']\gamma'\gamma$
	with $[\gamma,\gamma']\in\Gamma^{(k)}$,
	untwisted equivariance over $\Gamma^{(k)}$ gives
	$\lVert\mu_{\gamma\gamma'o}\rVert
	=\lVert\mu_{\gamma'\gamma o}\rVert$.
	Using the assumed twisted equivariance, we obtain
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		f_\gamma([\gamma'])
		&=\log\frac{\lVert\mu_{\gamma\gamma'o}\rVert}
		{\lVert\mu_{\gamma'o}\rVert}\\
		&=\log\frac{
			e^{\chi_{k-1}(\gamma)}\lVert\mu_{\gamma'o}\rVert}
		{\lVert\mu_{\gamma'o}\rVert}\\
		&=\chi_{k-1}(\gamma).
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Consequently, we have
	\[
	\chi(\gamma)=\mathfrak m(f_\gamma)
	=\chi_{k-1}(\gamma)
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}).
	\]
	Since $k\geq2$ and every character vanishes on commutators,
	\[
	\Gamma^{(k-1)}
	\subseteq\Gamma^{(1)}
	=\Gamma_0[\Gamma,\Gamma]
	\subseteq\ker\chi.
	\]
	Thus $\chi_{k-1}=\chi|_{\Gamma^{(k-1)}}=0$.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:descent}
	Let $\sigma>0$ and $\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma_0)$.
	If the $\Gamma_0$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is ergodic, then
	there is $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ with $\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0$
	such that
	\[
	\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\sigma,\Gamma),
	\qquad
	\sigma=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma).
	\]
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
Take the tower above with $n\geq1$. We prove by downward
	induction that
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)})$
	for $k=n,\ldots,1$.
	The case $k=n$ is the hypothesis.
	
	Suppose that $\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)})$
	with $k\geq2$. Since $\Gamma_0\subseteq\Gamma^{(k)}$,
	the $\Gamma^{(k)}$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is ergodic.
	Proposition~\ref{prop:quasiinv}, with $\chi=0$, makes the
	measure class of $\mu_o$ invariant under $\Gamma^{(k-1)}$.
	Applying Proposition~\ref{prop:emerging} to
	$(\Gamma^{(k)},\Gamma^{(k-1)})$ gives a character
	$\chi_{k-1}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma^{(k-1)},\mathbb{R})$, vanishing on
	$\Gamma^{(k)}$, such that
	\[
	\gamma_\ast\mu_x
	=e^{-\chi_{k-1}(\gamma)}\mu_{\gamma x}
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)},\ x\in\mathbb X).
	\]
	Lemma~\ref{lem:kill} gives $\chi_{k-1}=0$, hence
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k-1)})$.
	
	Thus $\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma^{(1)})$.
	Proposition~\ref{prop:quasiinv} at $k=1$ makes the measure
	class of $\mu_o$ $\Gamma$-invariant. Since the
	$\Gamma^{(1)}$-action is ergodic,
	Proposition~\ref{prop:emerging}, applied to
	$(\Gamma^{(1)},\Gamma)$, gives
	\[
	\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R}),
	\qquad
	\chi|_{\Gamma^{(1)}}=0,
	\qquad
	\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\sigma,\Gamma).
	\]
	Since $\Gamma_0\subseteq\Gamma^{(1)}$, we have
	$\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0$.
	Finally, Proposition~\ref{prop:rigidity} gives
	$\sigma=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:ascent}
	Let $\sigma>0$, $\chi\in\mathrm{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{R})$ with
	$\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0$, and
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\sigma,\Gamma)$.
	Then $\sigma=\delta_{\varphi,\chi}(\Gamma)$,
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma_0)$, and the
	$\Gamma_0$-action on $(\mathcal F,\mu_o)$ is ergodic.
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
The dimension equality follows from
	Proposition~\ref{prop:rigidity}, and
	$\mu\in\mathcal M_\varphi(\sigma,\Gamma_0)$ follows from
	\eqref{eq:equiv} and $\chi|_{\Gamma_0}=0$.
	      
	Let $A\subseteq\mathcal F$ be a $\Gamma_0$-invariant Borel set
	with $\mu_o(A)>0$, and set
	$\nu_x:=\mathbb1_A\mu_x$ for $x\in\mathbb X$.
	By \eqref{eq:conf},
	\[
	\nu_x=e^{-\sigma\varphi(\beta_\bullet(x,y))}\nu_y,
	\qquad
	0<\lVert\nu_x\rVert
	=\int_A e^{-\sigma\varphi(\beta_\xi(x,o))}\,d\mu_o(\xi)
	\leq\lVert\mu_x\rVert<\infty.
	\]
	Also, $\operatorname{supp}\nu_x\subseteq\Lambda_\Gamma$.
	    
	We prove by downward induction on $k=n,\ldots,0$ that
	\[
	\mu_o(\gamma A\triangle A)=0
	\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k)}).
	\]
	The case $k=n$ holds by hypothesis.
	Suppose it holds for some $k\geq1$.
	For $\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k)}$, \eqref{eq:conf} gives
	$\mu_{\gamma x}(\gamma A\triangle A)=0$.
	Hence, by \eqref{eq:equiv},
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\gamma_\ast\nu_x
		=\mathbb1_{\gamma A}\,\gamma_\ast\mu_x
		=e^{-\chi(\gamma)}\mathbb1_{\gamma A}\mu_{\gamma x}
		=e^{-\chi(\gamma)}\mathbb1_A\mu_{\gamma x}
		=e^{-\chi(\gamma)}\nu_{\gamma x}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Thus
	$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi|_{\Gamma^{(k)}}}
	(\sigma,\Gamma^{(k)})$.
	   
	For $\gamma\in\Gamma^{(k-1)}$, equation~\eqref{eq:equiv}
	also gives
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\nu_{\gamma o}
		=(\gamma^{-1})_\ast(\mathbb1_A\mu_{\gamma o})
		=\mathbb1_{\gamma^{-1}A}
		(\gamma^{-1})_\ast\mu_{\gamma o}
		=e^{\chi(\gamma)}\mathbb1_{\gamma^{-1}A}\mu_o.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Proposition~\ref{prop:quasiinv} therefore yields
	\[
	C_\gamma^{-1}\mathbb1_A\mu_o
	\leq e^{\chi(\gamma)}\mathbb1_{\gamma^{-1}A}\mu_o
	\leq C_\gamma\mathbb1_A\mu_o
	\]
	for some $C_\gamma\geq1$.
	Evaluating on $A^c$ and $(\gamma^{-1}A)^c$, respectively,
	gives
	\[
	\mu_o(\gamma^{-1}A\setminus A)
	=\mu_o(A\setminus\gamma^{-1}A)=0.
	\]
	Since $\gamma$ ranges over $\Gamma^{(k-1)}$, this proves
	the induction step.
	  
	At $k=0$, the same equivariance calculation gives
	$\nu\in\mathcal M_{\varphi,\chi}(\sigma,\Gamma)$.
	By Theorem~\ref{thm:HTS}\textup{(5)},
	\[
	\nu=c\mu,
	\qquad
	c=\frac{\mu_o(A)}{\lVert\mu_o\rVert}>0.
	\]
	Consequently,
	\[
	0=\nu_o(A^c)=c\,\mu_o(A^c),
	\]
	so $\mu_o(A^c)=0$, proving ergodicity.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:growthform}
		$\Theta_\Gamma\in\mathcal L^{>0}_\Gamma$, $\Theta_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}=\Theta_\Gamma$, and
		$\delta_{\Theta_\Gamma}(\Gamma)=1$.
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Since $\psi_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}=\psi_\Gamma$ and $\mathrm{i}$ is an isometry,
	$\Theta_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}\in D_\Gamma$ and
	$\lVert\Theta_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}\rVert=\lVert\Theta_\Gamma\rVert$.
	Uniqueness of the minimum-norm element therefore gives
	$\Theta_\Gamma\circ\mathrm{i}=\Theta_\Gamma$.
	
	By \cite[Cor.~3.3.5]{Quint02a}, there exists
	$u\in\mathcal L_\Gamma$ with $\lVert u\rVert=1$ and
	$\psi_\Gamma(u)>0$ such that
	$\Theta_\Gamma(v)=\psi_\Gamma(u)\langle u,v\rangle$
	for every $v\in\mathesstixfrak a$.
	For $v\in\mathesstixfrak a^+\smallsetminus\{0\}$, the coordinates of
	$u$ and $v$ are decreasing and sum to zero. Hence
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\Theta_\Gamma(v)
		&=\frac{\psi_\Gamma(u)}{d}
		\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d}(u_i-u_j)(v_i-v_j)\\
		&\geq\frac{\psi_\Gamma(u)}{d}
		(u_1-u_d)(v_1-v_d)
		>0.
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	Thus $\Theta_\Gamma\in\mathcal L^{>0}_\Gamma$.
	
	Finally, $\Theta_\Gamma\geq\psi_\Gamma$ and
	$\Theta_\Gamma(u)=\psi_\Gamma(u)>0$, so
	\eqref{eq:quint-sup} gives
	\[
	\delta_{\Theta_\Gamma}(\Gamma)
	=
	\sup_{v\in\mathcal L_\Gamma\smallsetminus\{0\}}
	\frac{\psi_\Gamma(v)}{\Theta_\Gamma(v)}
	=1.
	\]
<!-- end proof-39 -->
